     1	# CLAUDE.md
     2	
     3	> Repo renamed from zimage.swift to comfybox on 2026-05-08. Package and executable are now "ComfyBox"; the ZImage Swift module name is unchanged.
     4	
     5	This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     6	
     7	## Project Overview
     8	
     9	ComfyBox (formerly Z-Image.swift) is a Swift port of [Z-Image-Turbo](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo) using mlx-swift for Apple Silicon. It provides a CLI tool and library for text-to-image generation with support for LoRA and ControlNet.
    10	
    11	## Build Commands
    12	
    13	```bash
    14	# Build release CLI binary
    15	xcodebuild -scheme ComfyBox -configuration Release -destination 'platform=macOS' -derivedDataPath .build/xcode
    16	
    17	# Run all tests (use -enableCodeCoverage NO to avoid creating default.profraw)
    18	xcodebuild test -scheme comfybox-Package -destination 'platform=macOS' -enableCodeCoverage NO
    19	
    20	# Run specific test target
    21	xcodebuild test -scheme comfybox-Package -destination 'platform=macOS' -enableCodeCoverage NO -only-testing:ZImageTests
    22	
    23	# Run a single test class
    24	xcodebuild test -scheme comfybox-Package -destination 'platform=macOS' -enableCodeCoverage NO -only-testing:ZImageTests/FlowMatchSchedulerTests
    25	
    26	# Run a single test method
    27	xcodebuild test -scheme comfybox-Package -destination 'platform=macOS' -enableCodeCoverage NO -only-testing:ZImageTests/FlowMatchSchedulerTests/testTimestepsDecreasing
    28	```
    29	
    30	## Architecture
    31	
    32	### Core Components
    33	
    34	**Application Layer** (`Sources/ComfyBox`):
    35	- `ComfyBox`: Entry point for generation, controlnet, and quantization commands. Handles argument parsing and global GPU settings.
    36	
    37	**Pipeline Layer** (`Sources/ZImage/Pipeline`):
    38	- `ZImagePipeline`: Orchestrates Text-to-Image generation. Manages dynamic model loading/unloading (phase-scoped lifetimes), LoRA application, and the denoising loop.
    39	- `ZImageControlPipeline`: Extends generation with ControlNet conditioning (image/mask inputs) and Inpainting.
    40	- `FlowMatchScheduler`: Implements Flow Matching Euler scheduler with "Dynamic Shifting" for resolution-dependent noise schedules.
    41	
    42	**Model Layer** (`Sources/ZImage/Model`):
    43	- **TextEncoder**: Qwen-based transformer. Acts as both an Encoder (for conditioning) and a Generator (for Prompt Enhancement via LLM).
    44	- **Transformer**: The Diffusion Transformer (DiT).
    45	  - `ZImageTransformer2DModel`: Hybrid architecture with separate "refiner" streams (Noise & Context) that merge into joint attention blocks.
    46	  - `ZImageControlTransformer2DModel`: Adds ControlNet blocks to the base DiT architecture.
    47	- **VAE**: `AutoencoderKL` for encoding/decoding images to/from latents. Supports `AutoencoderDecoderOnly` for inference optimization.
    48	
    49	**Infrastructure** (`Sources/ZImage/Weights`, `/Quantization`, `/LoRA`):
    50	- **Weights**: Handles downloading from Hugging Face (`HubSnapshot`), parsing `.safetensors`, and detecting AIO checkpoints.
    51	- **Quantization**: `ZImageQuantizer` supports 4-bit and 8-bit group-wise quantization (Affine/MXFP4) for reduced memory footprint.
    52	- **LoRA**: `LoRAApplicator` supports both baked-in and dynamic (runtime) adapters, including LoKr (Kronecker product) and quantization compatibility.
    53	
    54	### Key Data Flow
    55	
    56	1. **Enhancement (Optional)**: Text prompt -> QwenTextEncoder (LLM Mode) -> Enhanced Prompt.
    57	2. **Encoding**: Enhanced Prompt -> QwenTokenizer -> QwenTextEncoder -> Text Embeddings.
    58	3. **Initialization**: Random Gaussian Latents generated.
    59	4. **Denoising Loop**:
    60	   - Latents + Timestep + Text Embeddings -> `ZImageTransformer2D` (Refiners -> Joint Blocks) -> Noise Prediction.
    61	   - `FlowMatchEulerScheduler` updates latents based on prediction.
    62	5. **Decoding**: Refined Latents -> `AutoencoderKL` (Decoder) -> RGB Image.
    63	
    64	### Test Structure
    65	
    66	- `Tests/ZImageTests/` - Unit tests (scheduler, config parsing, image I/O).
    67	- `Tests/ZImageIntegrationTests/` - Integration tests requiring model weights (pipeline, ControlNet, LoRA).
    68	- `Tests/ZImageE2ETests/` - End-to-end CLI tests (builds and runs the actual binary).
    69	
    70	## Requirements
    71	
    72	- macOS 14.0+ / iOS 16+
    73	- Apple Silicon
    74	- Swift 5.9+
     1	# SPEC: Kira reservation scheduler — typed media, timeslot packing, visible queue
     2	
     3	**Status:** draft from Todd's 2026-08-03 direction · **Owner:** Todd
     4	**Supersedes:** the count-based tick model ("N images + M videos per cycle")
     5	
     6	## 1. Todd's direction (verbatim inputs, 2026-08-03)
     7	
     8	1. "The Kira scheduler should be timeslot holders for stacked assets that
     9	   can fit within a given period. Images of a given tier or Video be it i2v
    10	   vs t2v should be sized to fit or be pushed. Each type should have a media
    11	   Type and be scheduled like a reservation system."
    12	2. "We have Animated portraits and Videos of action. Animated portraits are
    13	   i2v and t2v are Dreams or vignettes of given activity. Some i2v are
    14	   animations of action and others are more subtle. Each should be
    15	   qualifiable and optimized for delivery."
    16	3. "The Queue should show jobs that are stacked and pending. In the desktop
    17	   Application there doesn't seem to be a manageable queue — just the
    18	   current job."
    19	
    20	## 2. Concept model
    21	
    22	### MediaType — the qualifiable unit
    23	Every scheduled asset is an instance of a declared MediaType:
    24	
    25	| id | kind | engine path | recipe knobs (per-type optimization) |
    26	|---|---|---|---|
    27	| `image.<tier>` | still | krea2 preset per tier | preset id, dims, steps |
    28	| `portrait.animated` | i2v, SUBTLE | i2v, low motion | strength high-hold, low img_compression, gentle template ("breathing, blinking, hair drift"), 4–6s |
    29	| `animation.action` | i2v, ACTION | i2v, motion recipe | higher img_compression, action template w/ anatomy grounding, 8–10s |
    30	| `dream.vignette` | t2v | t2v scene | buildT2VScene prompt, 720p, 8–12s, cfg/NAG per validated recipe |
    31	
    32	**FPS is a per-type knob (Todd 2026-08-03):** playback fps rides the
    33	MediaType (engine accepts per-request fps today; frames = duration x fps
    34	snapped to the 1+8k grid, so fps directly scales render cost and must feed
    35	estCostSec). Distinct from `cond_fps` — the temporal-RoPE MOTION dial in the
    36	tuning block — which stays a recipe knob; the two are set independently
    37	(e.g. portrait.animated might render 16fps playback for a dreamy cadence
    38	while conditioning at model fps).
    39	
    40	- A MediaType is a **record, not code**: `{id, kind, engineParams (preset id +
    41	  LTX2VideoTuning block + template id), durationSeconds | dims, fps,
    42	  estCostSec, deliveryRules, tierEligibility}` — stored in kira config, editable from
    43	  the Kira tab. The engine-side knobs ride the machinery shipped today
    44	  (presets, videoTuning, prompt templates, per-request tuning).
    45	- "Qualifiable": every rendered asset carries its mediaType id into the
    46	  trace + gallery metadata, so ratings/QA aggregate BY TYPE ("action i2v is
    47	  drifting again") instead of by blob.
    48	- `estCostSec` starts as a config number per type and is then LEARNED: the
    49	  scheduler updates a rolling median from actual render durations (traces
    50	  already record elapsed).
    51	
    52	### Timeslots — the reservation ledger
    53	- A **cycle** (the existing pacing interval, e.g. 30 min) is a timeslot with
    54	  a capacity budget: `capacitySec = cycleLength − overheadReserve`.
    55	- The planner holds a standing **slot template** per tier window (e.g.
    56	  overnight banana: 1 dream.vignette + 2 image.banana + 1 portrait.animated),
    57	  also config, editable in the tab.
    58	- **Fit-or-push packing:** reservations are placed into the current slot in
    59	  priority order until `Σ estCostSec` would exceed capacity; the remainder
    60	  is PUSHED to the next slot's front (a persisted backlog, not dropped —
    61	  today's overflow just doesn't happen and vanishes).
    62	- Direct asks (Telegram/chat renders) preempt as today via the fast lane;
    63	  the slot's plan re-fits around the actual time consumed.
    64	- The ledger is persisted (survives daemon restarts; the orphan-reconciler
    65	  pattern applies).
    66	
    67	### Interactive windows — the responsiveness reserve (Todd, 2026-08-03)
    68	
    69	> As a User, I want to ask the Muse a question and have it answer within a
    70	> reasonable period of time.
    71	> **AC:** Muse responds when GPU avails; the scheduler leaves windows of
    72	> opportunity for responses.
    73	
    74	The packer never fills a slot wall-to-wall. Rules:
    75	
    76	- `capacitySec = cycleLength − overheadReserve − interactiveReserve`.
    77	- The interactive reserve is distributed as **windows**, not one lump: a
    78	  configurable cap on CONTIGUOUS GPU occupancy (default: no more than ~10
    79	  consecutive minutes of scheduled rendering without a gap ≥ one typical
    80	  chat-render, ~3 min). Long renders (dream.vignette at 12s ≈ 5–6 min) are
    81	  scheduled so a window follows them.
    82	- Direct chat/muse asks consume the interactive reserve via the existing
    83	  fast lane — they do NOT debit the slot's content budget (Q2: resolved).
    84	  Worst-case wait for a chat media reply = the remaining runtime of the
    85	  current job, bounded by the contiguous-occupancy cap.
    86	- Idle windows may be backfilled ONLY by a task that provably fits before
    87	  the window's end (a short image), else the GPU rests — an empty window is
    88	  the feature, not waste.
    89	- Text-only muse replies are unaffected (LLM path, no render GPU); this
    90	  reserve is about MEDIA responses.
    91	
    92	### Priority: on-demand bumps scheduled (Todd, 2026-08-03)
    93	
    94	> On-demand requests from User or Muse should bump queue.
    95	
    96	Two priority classes, strictly ordered:
    97	
    98	- **INTERACTIVE** — User asks (Telegram/chat/desktop) AND Muse-initiated
    99	  on-demand work (her suggestion-box picks, in-conversation renders).
   100	- **SCHEDULED** — the slot plan's reservations.
   101	
   102	Bump semantics: an INTERACTIVE job enters at the FRONT of the engine queue
   103	(the server's reorder capability, which already exists, becomes the
   104	mechanism); queued SCHEDULED jobs slide back and the ledger re-fits — spilled
   105	reservations go to the backlog per fit-or-push. The RUNNING job is never
   106	killed (renders aren't resumable): worst-case interactive wait stays
   107	bounded by the contiguous-occupancy cap. Combined with the cognition lease,
   108	this gives: text answers ≤ one denoise step; media answers ≤ current job's
   109	remainder; scheduled content never starves interactivity, only defers to it.
   110	
   111	### Shared-GPU contention — cognition vs generation (Todd, 2026-08-03)
   112	
   113	> Problem: GPU is a shared resource for LLM and Gen models. Turn responses
   114	> and cognition compete with image generations.
   115	
   116	Evidence: 2026-08-03 09:35 — Kira's agent loop timed out (90s) calling the
   117	LM Studio model mid-render. Windows between renders don't fix this: a
   118	single dream.vignette holds the GPU 5–6 minutes, and tokens starve INSIDE
   119	that span. Three layers, cheapest first:
   120	
   121	**Generalization (Todd): this holds for ANY model sharing the GPU** —
   122	vision/captioning (the planned VLM provider), embeddings, face detection,
   123	future audio models. The governor is therefore generic: every GPU consumer
   124	declares a class — `render` (long-occupancy, pausable at step boundaries)
   125	vs `inference` (short-burst, latency-sensitive: LLM turns, VLM captioning,
   126	embeddings, PROMPT OPTIMIZATION). Leases are the inference class's admission ticket; nothing is
   127	special-cased to "the chat LLM".
   128	
   129	1. **Inference lease (engine governor) — the real fix.** Renders are step
   130	   loops with existing per-step callbacks. Add a lease mechanism to the
   131	   warm server: `POST /v1/gpu/lease` (holder, ttl ≤ 90s) makes the active
   132	   render PAUSE AT THE NEXT STEP BOUNDARY and resume on release/expiry.
   133	   Bounded token latency = one denoise step (2–30s at production configs) +
   134	   LLM burst. The daemon takes a lease before any inference-class call during
   135	   active renders (LLM turn, VLM caption pass, embedding batch, prompt
   136	   optimization). Note the coupling: every scheduled render is PRECEDED by
   137	   an optimizer call, so the planner front-loads a slot's optimizer passes
   138	   into the opening window (batch-optimize, then render) instead of
   139	   interleaving each optimize against the previous item's render; leases are metered so renders still finish (max N leases
   140	   per render, else the render's own SLA dies).
   141	2. **Scheduler windows (already spec'd)** handle the render-class
   142	   interactive asks; they also lower the PROBABILITY of collision for
   143	   cognition, but are not sufficient alone.
   144	3. **Placement (structural, later):** cognition models contending on the
   145	   render GPU is ultimately a placement smell. Candidates: a small
   146	   cognition model on CPU for turn-critical paths (fallback when the lease
   147	   is unavailable), or ANE via CoreML for the fixed cognition model —
   148	   zero GPU contention. Measure before building: token latency during a
   149	   render at production configs is the number that decides whether layer 1
   150	   suffices.
   151	
   152	Measurement task (pre-build): LLM tokens/sec and time-to-first-token
   153	during (a) idle, (b) krea2 image render, (c) LTX 241f render — the
   154	contention curve that sizes the lease TTL and decides layer 3.
   155	
   156	### Queue visibility — the execution surface (Todd's #3)
   157	- The engine ALREADY has queue management server-side (pause/resume/reorder,
   158	  source attribution) and the desktop has a Queue tab — but stacked/pending
   159	  work is invisible in practice because (verify during build): video jobs
   160	  and daemon-side planned-but-not-yet-submitted reservations never appear;
   161	  only the in-flight job does.
   162	- Deliverable: the Queue tab shows THREE strata:
   163	  1. **Running** — current job with progress (exists)
   164	  2. **Queued in engine** — server FIFO with reorder/cancel (partially
   165	     exists; make video jobs appear)
   166	  3. **Reserved** — the daemon's slot plan for this + next cycle (new: GET
   167	     /v1/kira/schedule/ledger), each row typed (mediaType badge), with
   168	     push/pull/cancel controls
   169	- One timeline view = the reservation system made visible.
   170	
   171	## 3. Delivery optimization (Todd's "optimized for delivery")
   172	
   173	Per-MediaType delivery rules: target surface (Telegram tier stream, gallery
   174	only, suggestion-box reply), encode budget (bits/px — now request-tunable),
   175	and caption/notification behavior. E.g. portrait.animated may deliver as a
   176	looping short; dream.vignette as a full post.
   177	
   178	## 4. Phasing
   179	
   180	- **P1 (daemon):** MediaType records + slot planner + fit-or-push backlog +
   181	  ledger endpoint. The existing tick becomes "execute this slot's plan".
   182	  clipSeconds (shipped 2026-08-03) folds INTO per-type duration.
   183	- **P2 (desktop):** Queue tab strata + Kira tab MediaType/slot-template
   184	  editors (clipSeconds field arrives here, per-type).
   185	- **P3:** learned estCostSec, per-type QA dashboards from trace aggregation.
   186	
   187	## 5. Open questions for Todd
   188	
   189	1. Slot templates per tier window — who authors the starting set? (Propose:
   190	   I derive from current behavior — 4 images + 1–2 videos per 30min — and
   191	   you edit in the tab.)
   192	2. ~~Direct chat asks~~ RESOLVED 2026-08-03: they consume the interactive
   193	   reserve (windows between reservations), never the content budget; the
   194	   contiguous-occupancy cap bounds worst-case response latency.
   195	3. Backlog aging: does a pushed reservation expire after N slots, or
   196	   persist until rendered?
   197	4. portrait.animated vs animation.action selection: planner-scheduled
   198	   ratio, or content-driven (the muse/arc state decides)?
   199	
   200	## 6. Non-goals
   201	
   202	Multi-machine scheduling, external calendar integration, per-asset cost
   203	billing. The engine's FIFO stays the execution primitive — reservations
   204	plan ABOVE it, they don't replace it.

     1	# `Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo`
     2

     4	**Top-level files and folders** (authoritative list from the Hub API):
     5	`assets/`, `model_index.json`, `scheduler/`, `text_encoder/`, `tokenizer/`, `transformer/`, `vae/`, `README.md`, `.gitattributes`. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/api/models/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo)
     6	
     7	
     8	## `model_index.json` — the wiring diagram
     9	
    10	
    11	```json
    12	{
    13	  "_class_name": "ZImagePipeline",
    14	  "_diffusers_version": "0.36.0.dev0",
    15	  "scheduler": ["diffusers","FlowMatchEulerDiscreteScheduler"],
    16	  "text_encoder": ["transformers","Qwen3Model"],
    17	  "tokenizer": ["transformers","Qwen2Tokenizer"],
    18	  "transformer": ["diffusers","ZImageTransformer2DModel"],
    19	  "vae": ["diffusers","AutoencoderKL"]
    20	}
    21	
    22	```
    23	
    24	
    25	This instructs Diffusers to instantiate **ZImagePipeline** and load each component from its subfolder using the provided class. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/model_index.json)
    26	
    27	
    28	
    29	Note: Support for `ZImagePipeline`/`ZImageTransformer2DModel` was merged upstream in Diffusers (PRs #12703 and #12715). [GitHub+1](https://github.com/huggingface/diffusers/pull/12703)
    30	
    31	
    32	
    33	## `transformer/` — the DiT “backbone”
    34	
    35	
    36	- **Files:** `config.json`, `diffusion_pytorch_model-00001…00003.safetensors`, `diffusion_pytorch_model.safetensors.index.json`. Sharded weights total ~24.6 GB. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/tree/main/transformer)
    37	- **Class:** `ZImageTransformer2DModel`.
    38	**Key hyperparameters** (excerpt): `dim: 3840`, `n_layers: 30`, `n_heads: 30`, `n_kv_heads: 30`, `in_channels: 16`, `cap_feat_dim: 2560`, plus RoPE settings and S3-DiT-specific shape metadata (`axes_dims`, `axes_lens`, `all_patch_size`, etc.). [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/transformer/config.json)
    39	
    40	**What “Single-Stream” means here:** per the model card, **text tokens, “visual semantic” tokens, and VAE latent tokens are concatenated into a single sequence** processed by every DiT block—contrasting with dual-stream designs that cross-attend between separate text and image streams. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/README.md)
    41	
    42	
    43	## `text_encoder/` and `tokenizer/` — prompt encoding
    44	
    45	
    46	- **Text encoder class:** **Qwen3Model**, with architecture metadata indicating `Qwen3ForCausalLM` shape (e.g., `hidden_size: 2560`, `num_hidden_layers: 36`, `num_attention_heads: 32`), stored in three shards (`model-00001…00003.safetensors`). [Hugging Face+1](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/text_encoder/config.json)
    47	- **Tokenizer:** **Qwen2Tokenizer** with `tokenizer.json`, `tokenizer_config.json`, `vocab.json`, `merges.txt`. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/tree/main/tokenizer)
    48	
    49	
    50	Practical implication: unlike SD/SDXL (CLIP encoders) or FLUX variants (often T5-style encoders), Z-Image-Turbo uses a **Qwen-family LLM** as the text encoder, yielding larger, instruction-friendly embeddings.
    51	
    52	
    53	
    54	## `vae/` — latent codec
    55	
    56	
    57	- **Class:** `AutoencoderKL`, ~168 MB weights. **Config** exposes `latent_channels: 16`, `scaling_factor: 0.3611`, `shift_factor: 0.1159`, and `force_upcast: true`. Those scaling/shift constants are critical to obtain correct dynamic ranges when mapping latent space ↔ pixels. [Hugging Face+1](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/tree/main/vae)
    58	
    59	## `scheduler/` — sampler parameters
    60	
    61	
    62	- **Class:** `FlowMatchEulerDiscreteScheduler` with a minimal config (e.g., `num_train_timesteps`, `shift`, `use_dynamic_shifting`). The **few-step behavior** (≈8 forwards) comes from the **distilled model and runtime num_inference_steps**, not from the config alone. [Hugging Face+1](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/scheduler/scheduler_config.json)
    63	
    64	## `README.md` — usage and design notes worth keeping
    65	
    66	
    67	- **Architecture:** “Scalable **Single-Stream DiT**” (S3-DiT). [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/README.md)
    68	- **Few-step generation:** example uses `num_inference_steps=9` which leads to *8 DiT forwards*; set `guidance_scale=0.0` for Turbo variants. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/README.md)
    69	- **Environment:** recommends latest Diffusers (source install) and optional Flash-Attention backends. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/README.md)
    70	
    71	
    72	# How these pieces map to Diffusers concepts
    73	
    74	
    75	| Folder / File | Diffusers class (source) | Role in the pipeline |
    76	| ---- | ---- | ---- |
    77	| `model_index.json` | N/A (loader manifest) | Declares `_class_name` (`ZImagePipeline`) and maps component keys → libraries/classes. [Hugging Face+1](https://huggingface.co/docs/diffusers/v0.21.0/en/using-diffusers/loading?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
    78	| `transformer/…` | `ZImageTransformer2DModel` (`diffusers`) | **Noise/flow predictor** for latent tokens, operating on a **single concatenated sequence** of text + image tokens. [Hugging Face+1](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/transformer/config.json) |
    79	| `text_encoder/…` | `Qwen3Model` (`transformers`) | Encodes prompts into embeddings fed to the DiT (and to the “visual semantic” tokenizer path described in the paper/card). [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/text_encoder/config.json) |
    80	| `tokenizer/…` | `Qwen2Tokenizer` (`transformers`) | Produces input IDs for the Qwen3 encoder. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/tree/main/tokenizer) |
    81	| `vae/…` | `AutoencoderKL` (`diffusers`) | Compresses/decodes images ↔ latents; scaling/shift factors must be respected for numerically correct results. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/vae/config.json) |
    82	| `scheduler/…` | `FlowMatchEulerDiscreteScheduler` (`diffusers`) | Flow-matching sampler introduced post-SD3; determines the integration path over timesteps. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/docs/diffusers/en/api/schedulers/flow_match_euler_discrete?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
    83	
    84	
    85	
    86	# Cross-model perspective: DiT layouts are converging
    87	
    88	
    89	- **FLUX.1-dev**: `transformer/`, `vae/`, `scheduler/`, `text_encoder/` (+ sometimes `text_encoder_2`), `tokenizer/` (+ sometimes `tokenizer_2`), and `model_index.json`. Same pattern. [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev/tree/main)
    90	- **PixArt family** in Diffusers uses dedicated Transformer2D model classes under `transformers/` models directory (e.g., `pixart_transformer_2d.py`), again surfaced via `transformer/` in checkpoints. [GitHub](https://github.com/huggingface/diffusers/blob/main/src/diffusers/models/transformers/pixart_transformer_2d.py?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
    91	
    92	
    93	# Practical notes for users and evaluators
    94	
    95	
    96	1. **Sharding & indices.** Where weights exceed a few GB, expect `*-0000X-of-0000Y.safetensors` with a `*.index.json` that maps tensor names to shard files; Diffusers resolves this automatically via `from_pretrained`. Verified for both the **transformer** and **text_encoder** in Z-Image-Turbo. [Hugging Face+1](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/tree/main/transformer)
    97	2. **Few-step sampling.** The **8-forward** behavior comes from **distillation** + **FlowMatch** and the **runtime** `num_inference_steps`; follow the README recipe (`guidance_scale=0.0`, `num_inference_steps≈9`). [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/README.md)
    98	3. **Upstream support.** Use a recent Diffusers (the README points to source install) because **ZImagePipeline** and **ZImageTransformer2DModel** landed in late Nov-2025 PRs. [Hugging Face+1](https://huggingface.co/Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo/blob/main/README.md)

    25	 *     "imageCount": 2,

    27	 *     "activeHours": { "start": "20:00", "end": "08:00" }
    28	 *   }
    29	 * }
    30	 * ```
    31	 * `activeHours` is the content-creation window (server-local time; wraps
    32	 * midnight when start > end). ABSENT → the overnight default above. Explicit
    33	 * `null` → 24/7. The fruit mode is NOT config — it is read live from the
    34	 * content-mode store (`PUT /v1/kira/content-mode`, Kira tab / /apple etc.).
    35	 */
    36

    71	/** The 24/7 i2v clip length (Todd 2026-07-20). sulphur2/LTX-2 does ~12s single-pass. */
    72	// 8s renders as ONE single-pass i2v (193f) — no chunking, so no last-third color/
    73	// motion drift (Todd 2026-07-28; memory reference_kira_8s_video_recipe). 12s forced
    74	// 3 spliced chunks whose drift compounded into a degraded final third.
    75	export const I2V_CLIP_SECONDS = 8;  // default; policy clipSeconds overrides (4-12s single-pass window)
    76	export const CLIP_SECONDS_MIN = 4;
    77	export const CLIP_SECONDS_MAX = 12;  // 289f — beyond this is chunked continuation (seam risk)
    78	/** ComfyBox preset for the neutral art stream: bare Krea2, NO LoRAs. */
    79	export const NEUTRAL_ART_PRESET = 'krea2-base';
    80	/** ComfyBox preset for apple-tier 24/7 renders: identity LoRAs only — the
    81	 *  explicit-anatomy LoRA in krea-kira has no business in SFW output. */
    82	export const APPLE_SFW_PRESET = 'krea-kira-sfw';
    83	/** Max seed re-generations in the VLM adequacy loop before shipping best-effort. */
    84	export const SEED_QA_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 3;

    86	// ── Fruit mode ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    87	
    88	// Back-compat re-exports — the allowlist moved to stream-mode.ts (import-leaf)
    89	// so the tool/telegram/API surfaces can share it without an import cycle.
    90	export { SCHEDULER_CONTENT_MODES, normalizeSchedulerContentMode } from './stream-mode';
    91	export type { SchedulerContentMode } from './stream-mode';
    92	
    93	/**
    94	 * The stored default fruit mode — the same store the Kira-tab picker and
    95	 * /apple //banana //avocado write. Falls back to 'avocado' (the historical
    96	 * hardcode) only if the store itself is unreadable, never on a mere
    97	 * unfamiliar value (that normalizes to null → avocado too).
    98	 */
    99	export function resolveScheduledContentMode(): SchedulerContentMode {
   100	    try {
   101	        return normalizeSchedulerContentMode(kiraContentModeStore.getDefault()) ?? 'avocado';
   102	    } catch {
   103	        return 'avocado';
   104	    }
   105	}
   106	
   107	/** Which seed tiers a mode may draw from (cumulative; neutral never reaches
   108	 *  the Kira pools — it has its own still-life stream — but degrades to apple
   109	 *  defensively). */
   110	function tiersFor(mode: SchedulerContentMode): ReadonlyArray<'apple' | 'banana' | 'avocado'> {
   111	    switch (mode) {
   112	        case 'avocado': return ['apple', 'banana', 'avocado'];
   113	        case 'banana': return ['apple', 'banana'];
   114	        default: return ['apple'];
   115	    }
   116	}
   117	
   118	// ── Tiered schedule (v2, Todd 2026-07-27) ────────────────────────────────────
   119	
   120	/** One tier's schedule + pacing. Present in config.tiers = participates. */
   121	export interface TierConfig {
   122	    /** null = 24/7. On tolerant READ a missing/malformed value is 24/7 too
   123	     *  (never the old single-window overnight default — Codex B3). */
   124	    activeHours?: ActiveHours | null;
   125	    imageCount?: number;
   126	    unlimitedImages?: boolean;
   127	    /** Ignored (forced 0) for neutral — the Autocord shoots stills. */
   128	    videoCount?: number;
   129	}
   130	
   131	export type TierMap = Partial<Record<SchedulerContentMode, TierConfig>>;
   132	
   133	/** Overlap precedence: most explicit wins. */
   134	export const TIER_PRIORITY: readonly SchedulerContentMode[] = ['avocado', 'banana', 'apple', 'neutral'];
   135	
   136	/** Rotation walk order (Todd 2026-07-28 "all modes all day"): mild → explicit,
   137	 *  so an all-24/7 tier map cycles the full ladder instead of avocado winning
   138	 *  every slot. */
   139	export const TIER_ROTATION_ORDER: readonly SchedulerContentMode[] = ['neutral', 'apple', 'banana', 'avocado'];
   140	
   141	/** A present tier's window: explicit null/missing/malformed → 24/7. */
   142	export function tierWindow(tier: TierConfig): ActiveHours | null {
   143	    if (tier.activeHours === null || tier.activeHours === undefined) return null;
   144	    return normalizeActiveHours(tier.activeHours);
   145	}
   146	
   147	/** Clean a stored tier so what we SERVE always round-trips through PUT
   148	 *  validation (Codex impl-review W2: a malformed stored activeHours must
   149	 *  normalize to null on read, not 400 on the way back in). */
   150	export function sanitizeTier(mode: SchedulerContentMode, raw: TierConfig): TierConfig {
   151	    const out: TierConfig = { activeHours: tierWindow(raw) };
   152	    if (Number.isInteger(raw.imageCount) && Number(raw.imageCount) >= 0) out.imageCount = Number(raw.imageCount);
   153	    if (raw.unlimitedImages === true) out.unlimitedImages = true;
   154	    if (mode !== 'neutral' && Number.isInteger(raw.videoCount) && Number(raw.videoCount) >= 0) {
   155	        out.videoCount = Number(raw.videoCount);
   156	    }
   157	    return out;
   158	}
   159	
   160	/** The tier legacy flat clients see and edit (Codex impl-review B1): avocado
   161	 *  when present, else the highest-priority present tier (i.e. the migrated
   162	 *  one). Old flat PUTs must keep steering the tier that is actually running —
   163	 *  NOT conjure a 24/7 avocado tier that would win every cycle. */
   164	export function legacyProjectionTier(tiers: TierMap): SchedulerContentMode {
   165	    if (tiers.avocado) return 'avocado';
   166	    for (const mode of TIER_PRIORITY) {
   167	        if (tiers[mode]) return mode;
   168	    }
   169	    return 'avocado';
   170	}
   171	
   172	/** Pacing for a chosen mode; falls back to defaults when the tier isn't
   173	 *  configured (an override can select an unscheduled tier). */
   174	export function tierPacing(mode: SchedulerContentMode, tier: TierConfig | undefined): {
   175	    imageCount: number; unlimitedImages: boolean; videoCount: number;
   176	} {
   177	    return {
   178	        imageCount: Number.isInteger(tier?.imageCount) ? Number(tier?.imageCount) : 2,
   179	        unlimitedImages: tier?.unlimitedImages === true,
   180	        videoCount: mode === 'neutral' ? 0 : (Number.isInteger(tier?.videoCount) ? Number(tier?.videoCount) : 1),
   181	    };
   182	}
   183	
   184	/**
   185	 * Effective tier map. Config with `tiers` → as stored. Legacy flat config →
   186	 * BEHAVIOR-PRESERVING synthesis (Codex B1): one tier keyed by the content-mode
   187	 * store's CURRENT default (the store steered v1's mode), carrying the flat
   188	 * window/pacing. v1's absent-window semantics (overnight default) apply to the
   189	 * synthesized tier only.
   190	 */
   191	export function resolveTierMap(
   192	    config: ContentSchedulerConfig,
   193	    // Injectable for deterministic tests — production reads the store.
   194	    legacyModeRaw: string = resolveScheduledContentMode(),
   195	): TierMap {
   196	    if (config.tiers && typeof config.tiers === 'object') {
   197	        const out: TierMap = {};
   198	        for (const mode of SCHEDULER_CONTENT_MODES) {
   199	            const t = (config.tiers as Record<string, unknown>)[mode];
   200	            if (t && typeof t === 'object') out[mode] = sanitizeTier(mode, t as TierConfig);
   201	        }
   202	        return out;
   203	    }
   204	    const legacyMode = normalizeSchedulerContentMode(legacyModeRaw) ?? 'avocado';
   205	    return {
   206	        [legacyMode]: {
   207	            activeHours: resolveActiveHours(config.activeHours),
   208	            imageCount: config.imageCount ?? 2,
   209	            unlimitedImages: config.unlimitedImages === true,
   210	            videoCount: config.videoCount ?? 1,
   211	        },
   212	    };
   213	}
   214	
   215	/** Tiers whose window contains `date`, highest priority first. */
   216	export function openTiersAt(date: Date, tiers: TierMap): SchedulerContentMode[] {
   217	    return TIER_PRIORITY.filter((mode) => {
   218	        const t = tiers[mode];
   219	        return t !== undefined && isWithinActiveHours(date, tierWindow(t));
   220	    });
   221	}
   222	
   223	export interface ResolvedCycle {
   224	    mode: SchedulerContentMode;
   225	    modeSource: 'override-todd' | 'override-kira' | 'schedule';
   226	    pacing: { imageCount: number; unlimitedImages: boolean; videoCount: number };
   227	    /** The winning tier's window when schedule-driven (chain clamp); null for
   228	     *  overrides — they have no window edge. */
   229	    window: ActiveHours | null;
   230	}
   231	
   232	/** Rotation state for a schedule-driven pick: walk TIER_ROTATION_ORDER across
   233	 *  the open tiers, continuing after `prevMode` (the last schedule-driven
   234	 *  cycle's tier; null or no-longer-open restarts at the first open tier). */
   235	export interface TierRotation {
   236	    enabled?: boolean;
   237	    prevMode?: SchedulerContentMode | null;
   238	}
   239	
   240	/**
   241	 * Per-cycle mode resolution — precedence todd > kira > schedule. Among open
   242	 * tiers the schedule takes the most explicit, or with rotation enabled
   243	 * (`tierRotation`, Todd 2026-07-28 "all modes all day") the next open tier
   244	 * after `rotation.prevMode` in mild→explicit order. Returns null when
   245	 * schedule-driven and nothing is open (the cycle is skipped).
   246	 */
   247	export function resolveCycle(date: Date, tiers: TierMap, rotation?: TierRotation): ResolvedCycle | null {
   248	    const stream = loadStreamMode();
   249	    if (stream.todd) {
   250	        return {
   251	            mode: stream.todd, modeSource: 'override-todd',
   252	            pacing: tierPacing(stream.todd, tiers[stream.todd]), window: null,
   253	        };
   254	    }
   255	    if (stream.kira) {
   256	        return {
   257	            mode: stream.kira, modeSource: 'override-kira',
   258	            pacing: tierPacing(stream.kira, tiers[stream.kira]), window: null,
   259	        };
   260	    }
   261	    const open = openTiersAt(date, tiers);
   262	    if (open.length === 0) return null;
   263	    let mode = open[0];
   264	    if (rotation?.enabled === true) {
   265	        const order = TIER_ROTATION_ORDER.filter((m) => open.includes(m));
   266	        const prevIdx = rotation.prevMode ? order.indexOf(rotation.prevMode) : -1;
   267	        mode = order[(prevIdx + 1) % order.length];
   268	    }
   269	    return {
   270	        mode, modeSource: 'schedule',
   271	        pacing: tierPacing(mode, tiers[mode]), window: tierWindow(tiers[mode]!),
   272	    };
   273	}
   274	
   275	// ── Rotation cursor persistence (Todd 2026-07-28 follow-up) ─────────────────
   276	// The cursor was in-memory only, so every deploy restart reset the walk to
   277	// neutral — on an active deploy day the schedule never got past the first
   278	// tier ("current render seems to be the old neutral variety"). Persist the
   279	// last schedule-driven mode in BREE_HOME so restarts CONTINUE the walk.
   280	
   281	const ROTATION_CURSOR_FILE = 'rotation-cursor.json';
   282	
   283	/** Load the persisted rotation cursor; null when missing/corrupt/invalid. */
   284	export function loadRotationCursor(): SchedulerContentMode | null {
   285	    try {
   286	        const raw = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(breeHome(), ROTATION_CURSOR_FILE), 'utf-8'));
   287	        return normalizeSchedulerContentMode(raw?.prevMode) ?? null;
   288	    } catch {
   289	        return null;
   290	    }
   291	}
   292	
   293	/** Persist the rotation cursor. Fail-soft — a lost cursor only costs one
   294	 *  restart re-starting the walk, never a render. */
   295	export function saveRotationCursor(mode: SchedulerContentMode): void {
   296	    try {
   297	        fs.writeFileSync(
   298	            path.join(breeHome(), ROTATION_CURSOR_FILE),
   299	            JSON.stringify({ prevMode: mode }),
   300	        );
   301	    } catch { /* fail-soft */ }
   302	}
   303	
   304	/** The shared status shape (Codex W5) — tool / /stream / API all serve this. */
   305	export function buildStreamStatus(now: Date = new Date()): StreamStatus {
   306	    const scheduler = activeScheduler;
   307	    const config = scheduler ? scheduler.currentConfig() : loadSchedulerConfigForStatus();
   308	    const tiers = resolveTierMap(config);
   309	    const stream = loadStreamMode();
   310	    const open = openTiersAt(now, tiers);
   311	    // Rotation-aware preview: with tierRotation on, effectiveMode is the tier
   312	    // the NEXT schedule-driven cycle will actually pick (status must not lie).
   313	    const resolved = resolveCycle(now, tiers, {
   314	        enabled: config.tierRotation === true,
   315	        prevMode: scheduler?.lastScheduledMode() ?? null,
   316	    });
   317	    return {
   318	        effectiveMode: resolved?.mode ?? null,
   319	        source: resolved
   320	            ? resolved.modeSource
   321	            : 'none-open',
   322	        todd: stream.todd,
   323	        kira: stream.kira,
   324	        openTiers: open,
   325	        paused: fs.existsSync(path.join(breeHome(), 'scheduler-paused')),
   326	        enabled: config.enabled !== false,
   327	    };
   328	}
   329	
   330	/** Status fallback when no live scheduler is registered (tests, early boot).
   403	export interface ContentSchedulerConfig {
   404	    enabled?: boolean;
   405	    intervalMinutes?: number;      // default 30
   406	    imageCount?: number;           // images per cycle, default 2
   407	    videoCount?: number;           // videos per cycle, default 1
   408	    /** i2v (base→animate), t2v (text→scene), or mixed (both, per video). Default 'mixed'. */
   409	    videoMode?: 'i2v' | 't2v' | 'mixed';
   410	    /**
   411	     * Unlimited-within-cycle images (Todd 2026-07-20): instead of a fixed
   412	     * imageCount, keep generating for the whole cycle window — each render
   413	     * chains the next one only while the window is open, so throughput
   414	     * adapts to actual render times and the single-flight GPU queue never
   415	     * backs up into the next cycle (no logjams). imageCount is ignored
   416	     * while this is true.
   417	     */
   418	    unlimitedImages?: boolean;
   419	    /**
   420	     * LEGACY single-window (v1) — used only when `tiers` is absent, feeding
   421	     * the behavior-preserving migration in resolveTierMap. Absent →
   422	     * DEFAULT_ACTIVE_HOURS (overnight); explicit null → 24/7.
   423	     */
   424	    activeHours?: ActiveHours | null;
   425	    /**
   426	     * v2 canonical shape: per-tier schedule + pacing. Present ⇒ the flat
   427	     * pacing fields above are ignored (the API persists tiers-only).
   428	     */
   429	    tiers?: TierMap;
   430	    /**
   431	     * Round-robin the schedule-driven pick across OPEN tiers (mild→explicit)
   432	     * instead of most-explicit-wins, so overlapping/24-7 tiers all render
   433	     * across the day (Todd 2026-07-28 "all modes all day"). Default false.
   434	     */
   435	    tierRotation?: boolean;
   436	    /**
   437	     * Arc lifecycle durations (Inner Loop F5): arcs age fresh → evolving →
   438	     * finale → retired from createdAt. Hand-edited config — NOT on the policy
   439	     * PUT surface (the canonical rewrite preserves it); absent → PRD defaults
   440	     * {freshDays: 3, evolveDays: 4}.
   441	     */
   442	    arcLifecycle?: ArcLifecycleConfig;
   443	}
   444	
   445	interface ArcState {
   446	    streakDays?: number;
   447	    mood?: string;
   448	    positiveRate?: number;
   449	    /** Set by the (backlog #1270) film-aesthetic path; 'film' forces 1:1 square,
   450	     *  normal-FOV framing (Minolta Autocord). Absent today → digital alternation. */
   451	    cameraAesthetic?: string;
   452	}
   453	
   454	export class KiraContentScheduler implements EventSource {
   455	    readonly name = 'kira_content_scheduler';
   456	    private config: ContentSchedulerConfig;
   457	    private intervalId: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null;
   458	    private lastGeneratedAt = 0;
   459	    /** Last schedule-driven tier — rotation cursor, persisted in BREE_HOME so
   460	     *  deploy restarts CONTINUE the walk (2026-07-28 follow-up: in-memory-only
   461	     *  reset the walk to neutral on every restart). Overrides don't advance it. */
   462	    private lastScheduleMode: SchedulerContentMode | null = null;
   463	
   464	    constructor(private pushEvent: (event: DaemonEvent) => void) {
   465	        this.config = this.loadConfig();
   466	        this.lastScheduleMode = loadRotationCursor();
   467	    }
   468	
   469	
   470	
   471	    private loadConfig(): ContentSchedulerConfig {
   472	        try {
   473	            const raw = JSON.parse(
   474	                fs.readFileSync(
   475	                    path.join(breeHome(), 'config.json'),
   476	                    'utf-8',
   477	                ),
   478	            );
   479	            return raw?.contentScheduler ?? { enabled: true, intervalMinutes: 30, imageCount: 2, videoCount: 1 };
   480	        } catch {
   481	            return { enabled: true, intervalMinutes: 30, imageCount: 2, videoCount: 1 };
   482	        }
   483	    }
   484	
   485	    private loadArcState(): ArcState {
   486	        try {
   487	            const arc = JSON.parse(
   488	                fs.readFileSync(
   489	                    path.join(breeHome(), 'inner-life', 'arc.json'),
   490	                    'utf-8',
   491	                ),
   492	            );
   493	            return {
   494	                streakDays: Number(arc?.streakDays) || 0,
   495	                mood: arc?.currentMood || 'neutral',
   496	                positiveRate: arc?.positiveRate || 0.5,
   497	            };
   498	        } catch {
   499	            return { streakDays: 0, mood: 'neutral', positiveRate: 0.5 };
   500	        }
   501	    }
   502	
   503	    async start(): Promise<void> {
   504	        if (!this.config.enabled) return;
   505	
   506	        // Idempotency guard (#kira-scheduler-double-start): start() is invoked
   507	        // twice in production — once by initKiraContentScheduler() and again by
   508	        // the daemon's module-source lifecycle loop. Without this early return
   509	        // the second call scheduled a SECOND setInterval and orphaned the first
   510	        // (overwriting this.intervalId), so tick() fired twice per cycle. If a
   511	        // timer is already live, do nothing. stop() nulls intervalId, so a
   512	        // genuine restart after stop() is still allowed.
   513	        if (this.intervalId !== null) return;
   514	
   515	        const intervalMs = (this.config.intervalMinutes ?? 30) * 60_000;
   516	
   517	        // Run initial check after short delay
   518	        setTimeout(() => this.tick(), 5000);
   519	
   520	        // Then run on the fixed interval
   521	        this.intervalId = setInterval(() => this.tick(), intervalMs);
   522	    }
   523	
   524	    stop(): void {
   525	        if (this.intervalId !== null) {
   526	            clearInterval(this.intervalId);
   527	            this.intervalId = null;
   528	        }
   529	    }
   530	
   531	    /**
   532	     * Live pause lever: a `scheduler-paused` file in breeHome() halts the 24/7
   533	     * content cycle without a daemon restart (tick never re-reads config). Any
   534	     * error (fs unavailable, etc.) is swallowed → not paused, never throws.
   535	     */
   536	    private isPaused(): boolean {
   537	        try {
   538	            return fs.existsSync(path.join(breeHome(), 'scheduler-paused'));
   539	        } catch {
   540	            return false;
   541	        }
   542	    }
   543	
   544	    /**
   545	     * Reconcile the tick timer with the (re-read) policy. Called by the
   546	     * policy PUT route (via notifyContentSchedulerPolicyChanged) so an
   547	     * enabled flip takes effect IMMEDIATELY — previously the timer only ever
   548	     * started at construction, so enabling a daemon that booted with
   549	     * enabled=false did nothing until a restart (timer-start bug, 2026-07-25).
   550	     * Idempotent: reload config, then start/stop only on an actual mismatch.
   551	     */
   552	    applyPolicyChange(): void {
   553	        const previousInterval = this.config.intervalMinutes ?? 30;
   554	        this.config = { ...this.config, ...this.loadConfig() };
   555	        if (this.config.enabled && this.intervalId === null) {
   556	            console.log('[kira-scheduler] policy enabled — starting tick loop');
   557	            void this.start();
   558	        } else if (!this.config.enabled && this.intervalId !== null) {
   559	            console.log('[kira-scheduler] policy disabled — stopping tick loop');
   560	            this.stop();
   561	        } else if (this.intervalId !== null
   562	                   && (this.config.intervalMinutes ?? 30) !== previousInterval) {
   563	            // Live cadence change (Codex W7): the timer used to be pinned to
   564	            // its start() interval until a daemon restart.
   565	            const intervalMs = (this.config.intervalMinutes ?? 30) * 60_000;
   566	            clearInterval(this.intervalId);
   567	            this.intervalId = setInterval(() => this.tick(), intervalMs);
   568	            console.log(`[kira-scheduler] tick interval rescheduled to ${this.config.intervalMinutes}m`);
   569	        }
   570	    }
   571	
   572	    /** The live config (status surface). */
   573	    currentConfig(): ContentSchedulerConfig {
   574	        return this.config;
   575	    }
   576	
   577	    /** Rotation cursor for status preview (buildStreamStatus). */
   578	    lastScheduledMode(): SchedulerContentMode | null {
   579	        return this.lastScheduleMode;
   580	    }
   581	
   582	    /**
   583	     * Stream-mode slot changed (tool / /stream / API): reset the double-fire
   584	     * gate and tick NOW so overrides take effect within seconds, not at the
   585	     * next interval (Codex W6). An already-running unlimited chain is not
   586	     * pre-empted — it finishes its cycle at the stamped tier.
   587	     */
   588	    pokeStreamModeChanged(): void {
   589	        this.lastGeneratedAt = 0;
   590	        this.tick();
   591	    }
   592	
   593	    private tick(): void {
   594	        // Checked BEFORE the lastGeneratedAt gate so a paused cycle is skipped
   595	        // entirely and does not advance lastGeneratedAt.
   596	        if (this.isPaused()) {
   597	            console.log('[kira-scheduler] paused (scheduler-paused file present) — skipping cycle');
   598	            return;
   599	        }
   600	
   601	        // Live policy: re-read config every cycle so PUT
   602	        // /v1/kira/content-scheduler/policy edits (Kira tab) take effect on
   603	        // the next tick without a daemon restart. The interval TIMER stays as
   604	        // started; intervalMinutes changes apply to window math immediately
   605	        // and to the timer on the next daemon restart.
   606	        this.config = { ...this.config, ...this.loadConfig() };
   607	
   608	        // Honor a live disable (hand-edited config or a PUT the notifier
   609	        // missed): a disabled scheduler must not keep generating. stop() nulls
   610	        // intervalId, so a later enabled=true policy update can restart it.
   611	        if (this.config.enabled === false) {
   612	            console.log('[kira-scheduler] disabled by policy — stopping tick loop');
   613	            this.stop();
   614	            return;
   615	        }
   616	
   617	        // Tiered resolution (v2, Todd 2026-07-27): precedence todd override >
   618	        // Kira's choice > schedule (per-tier windows, most explicit wins —
   619	        // or the rotation walk when tierRotation is on). Checked before the
   620	        // lastGeneratedAt gate so a skipped slot never counts as "generated".
   621	        const tiers = resolveTierMap(this.config);
   622	        const cycle = resolveCycle(new Date(), tiers, {
   623	            enabled: this.config.tierRotation === true,
   624	            prevMode: this.lastScheduleMode,
   625	        });
   626	        if (cycle === null) {
   627	            console.log('[kira-scheduler] no tier window open (and no override) — skipping cycle');
   628	            return;
   629	        }
   630	        const now = Date.now();
   631	        const intervalMs = (this.config.intervalMinutes ?? 30) * 60_000;
   632	
   633	        // Skip if already generated recently (prevents double-fire)
   634	        if (now - this.lastGeneratedAt < intervalMs * 0.9) return;
   635	
   636	        this.lastGeneratedAt = now;
   637	
   638	        // Advance + persist the rotation cursor only for a cycle that actually
   639	        // FIRES — a double-fire-gated tick must not step the walk (that would
   640	        // skip tiers), and the persisted cursor lets a deploy restart continue
   641	        // the walk instead of resetting to neutral (2026-07-28 follow-up).
   642	        if (cycle.modeSource === 'schedule') {
   643	            this.lastScheduleMode = cycle.mode;
   644	            saveRotationCursor(cycle.mode);
   645	        }
   646	
   647	        const { imageCount, unlimitedImages, videoCount } = cycle.pacing;
   648	        const imageLabel = unlimitedImages ? 'unlimited (fit to cycle)' : String(imageCount);
   649	
   650	        // Emit generation event. `modeSource` — NOT `source` — the top-level
   651	        // DaemonEvent.source stays the handler's discriminator (Codex B4).
   652	        this.pushEvent({
   653	            id: generateId(),
   654	            source: 'kira_content_scheduler',
   655	            summary: `Generate ${cycle.mode} content (${cycle.modeSource}): ${imageLabel} images + ${videoCount} videos`,
   656	            details: JSON.stringify({
   657	                imageCount,
   658	                unlimitedImages,
   659	                intervalMinutes: this.config.intervalMinutes ?? 30,
   660	                videoCount,
   661	                // Default 'i2v', NOT 'mixed': un-pinning t2v (2026-07-28) must
   662	                // not flip behavior for configs that never set videoMode.
   663	                videoMode: this.config.videoMode ?? 'i2v',
   664	                clipSeconds: (this.config as { clipSeconds?: number }).clipSeconds,
   665	                contentMode: cycle.mode,
   666	                modeSource: cycle.modeSource,
   667	                activeHours: cycle.window,
   668	                promptMethod: 'jit-local-model',
   669	                videoMethod: this.config.videoMode ?? 'i2v',
   670	                timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
   671	            }),
   672	            priority: 'low' as EventPriority,
   673	            createdAt: Date.now(),
   674	        });
   675	    }
   676	}
   677	
   678	// ── Live policy notifier ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   679	// The policy PUT route (kira-api.ts) persists config.json but has no handle on
   680	// the scheduler instance the daemon lifecycle owns. This tiny registry gives it
   681	// one: init registers the live instance; the route calls the notifier after a
   682	// successful write so an enabled false→true flip starts the tick loop NOW (and
   683	// true→false stops it) instead of waiting for a daemon restart.
   684	
   685	let activeScheduler: KiraContentScheduler | null = null;
   686	
   687	/** Register (or clear, with null) the live scheduler instance. */
   688	export function setActiveContentScheduler(scheduler: KiraContentScheduler | null): void {
   689	    activeScheduler = scheduler;
   690	}
   691	
   692	/** Tell the live scheduler its policy changed (no-op when none registered). */
   693	export function notifyContentSchedulerPolicyChanged(): void {
   694	    activeScheduler?.applyPolicyChange();
   695	}
   696	
   697	/**
   698	 * Event handler: kira_generate_content
   699	 * Generates content at the event's fruit mode (falling back to the live store
   700	 * default) with local model prompts and i2v videos. Neutral mode is the
   701	 * Autocord art-photography stream: film still lifes, no character injection,
   702	 * images only.
   703	 */
   704	export async function handleKiraContentGenerationEvent(
   705	    event: DaemonEvent,
   706	    ctx: PersonaTurnContext,
   707	): Promise<void> {
   708	    if (event.source !== 'kira_content_scheduler') return;
   709	
   710	    let data: any = {};
   711	    try {
   712	        data = JSON.parse(event.details ?? '{}');
   713	    } catch {
   714	        // fall through to defaults
   715	    }
   716	    const unlimitedImages = data.unlimitedImages === true;
   717	    const imageCount = data.imageCount ?? 2;
   718	    const videoCount = data.videoCount ?? 1;
   719	    const intervalMinutes = Number.isFinite(Number(data.intervalMinutes))
   720	        ? Number(data.intervalMinutes)
   721	        : 30;
   722	    // Absent → 'i2v' (backward-compatible default for older events/tests). The
   723	    // live scheduler emits 'mixed' by config default, so production gets both.
   724	    const videoMode: string = data.videoMode ?? 'i2v';
   725	    const clipSeconds = resolveClipSeconds((data as { clipSeconds?: number }).clipSeconds);
   726	    // Fruit mode: the event's stamp wins (what the cycle announced); an event
   727	    // without one (older queue entries, tests) resolves the live store default.
   728	    const contentMode: SchedulerContentMode =
   729	        normalizeSchedulerContentMode(data.contentMode) ?? resolveScheduledContentMode();
   730	    // Neutral is Kira's ART stream, not SFW-Kira: Autocord film still lifes,
   731	    // 6×6 square, her description NEVER enters the prompt, and no videos (a
   732	    // TLR shoots stills).
   733	    const filmStream = contentMode === 'neutral';
   734	    // Window clamp for the unlimited chain. The tick gate is the real window
   735	    // enforcement (it stamps activeHours on every event it emits); an event
   736	    // WITHOUT the field (pre-deploy queue entries, tests) clamps nothing.
   737	    const activeHours = 'activeHours' in data ? resolveActiveHours(data.activeHours) : null;
   738	
   739	    console.log(
   740	        `[kira-content-scheduler] Enqueuing ${unlimitedImages ? 'unlimited (fit to cycle)' : imageCount} images + ${filmStream ? 0 : videoCount} videos (${videoMode}) — ${contentMode} — off-turn`,
   741	    );
   742	
   743	    // Load arc state to inform prompt generation
   744	    const arcState = loadArcState();
   745	
   746	    // Arc lifecycle (F5): read live once per cycle — same tolerant config
   747	    // read the status snapshot uses — so a duration edit applies next cycle
   748	    // without a daemon restart. The SAME config must govern EVERY
   749	    // activeArcTexts evaluation this cycle (muse tick included): that helper
   750	    // write-through-retires arcs under whatever config it is handed, so a
   751	    // default-config call ahead of planCycleSuggestions would permanently
   752	    // retire arcs the configured longer lifecycle still considers live.
   753	    const arcLifecycle = loadSchedulerConfigForStatus().arcLifecycle;
   754	
   755	    // F2 muse tick (Inner Loop, PRD §3 F2): her solitude generates ideas — a
   756	    // thin box (< 3 pending usable by this tier) plus a stale tick (> 6h,
   757	    // persisted) triggers ONE sealed local-LLM call that files 1-3
   758	    // kira-authored ideas BEFORE the cycle plans its seeds, so a fresh idea
   759	    // can seed THIS cycle. Fire-and-await under try/catch (review checklist
   760	    // 3 — a muse failure must never cost a render cycle); the time budget is
   761	    // the optimizer client's own timeoutMs, no second timer.
   762	    try {
   763	        const muse = await runScheduledMuseTick(contentMode, { arcLifecycle });
   764	        if (muse.ran) {
   765	            console.log(`[kira-content-scheduler] Muse tick: ${muse.added} idea(s) filed${muse.reason ? ` (${muse.reason})` : ''}`);
   766	        }
   767	    } catch (err) {
   768	        console.warn(`[kira-content-scheduler] Muse tick failed (${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}) — proceeding`);
   769	    }
   770	
   771	    // Canonical model sketches (Todd 2026-07-29): the Characters tab (engine
   772	    // /v1/characters) owns Divine/Rhea/Bea's descriptions — pull them once per
   773	    // erotic-film-eligible cycle. FIRE-AND-FORGET, deliberately not awaited:
   774	    // this cycle's draws use the last refresh's cache (code fallbacks on the
   775	    // very first), and the cycle never spends its window on network — the
   776	    // gate's unlimited-chain timing test caught an awaited version stalling
   777	    // cycle start behind a real fetch.
   778	    if (contentMode === 'banana' || contentMode === 'avocado') {
   779	        void refreshEroticFilmModels();
   780	    }
   781	
   782	    // Suggestion box (Todd 2026-07-17): pending image/video ideas replace the
   783	    // random seeds FIFO (consumed); a session idea + active arcs theme every
   784	    // seed this cycle. See src/kira/suggestions.ts for the semantics.
   785	    // Unlimited mode consumes up to 6 image ideas; the chain falls back to
   786	    // generated intents past the suggestion supply.
   787	    const plan = planCycleSuggestions(unlimitedImages ? 6 : imageCount, videoCount,
   788	                                      undefined, undefined, {
   789	                                          tier: contentMode,
   790	                                          // Arc lifecycle (F5): the cycle's single
   791	                                          // live read, shared with the muse tick
   792	                                          // above (see that comment).
   793	                                          arcLifecycle,
   794	                                      });
   795	    // Inspiration well (Todd 2026-07-29 "the box should always serve as
   796	    // inspiration for random creativity"): a cycle with no explicit theme
   797	    // (no session idea, no active arc for this tier) draws ONE entry from the
   798	    // whole box — pending or already-consumed, weighted toward the least-used
   799	    // — so past ideas keep sparking new cycles instead of dying at consumption.
   800	    if (!plan.theme) {
   801	        const insp = drawInspiration(undefined, { tier: contentMode });
   802	        if (insp) {
   803	            plan.theme = insp.text;
   804	            console.log(`[kira-content-scheduler] Inspiration well: themed cycle from "${insp.text.slice(0, 60)}" (${insp.id})`);
   805	        }
   806	    }
   807	    if (plan.imageSeeds.length > 0 || plan.videoSeeds.length > 0 || plan.theme) {
   808	        console.log(
   809	            `[kira-content-scheduler] Suggestion box: ${plan.imageSeeds.length} image, `
   810	            + `${plan.videoSeeds.length} video idea(s), theme=${plan.theme ?? 'none'}`,
   810	            + `${plan.videoSeeds.length} video idea(s), theme=${plan.theme ?? 'none'}`,
   811	        );
   812	    }
   813	
   814	    // CRITICAL (#1158 render-queue substrate): renders take 130-275s each, far
   815	    // over the 180s ThreadMailbox turn cap. Awaiting them inline timed out the
   816	    // turn and orphaned every video cycle. Instead we enqueueRender — which
   817	    // returns immediately, so this handler's turn completes in milliseconds —
   818	    // and the single-flight GPU queue drains the jobs serially OFF-TURN (up to
   819	    // the 600s tool timeout). Same substrate the async-envelope + nightly
   820	    // media pool ride on; the one Mac GPU stays serialized across all producers.
   821	    // Truthful success signal (2026-07-25): a render only COUNTS when the tool
   822	    // returned a real output file — previously executeLocal failures (comfybox
   823	    // unreachable, LoRA swap failed, …) resolved to `undefined`-ish payloads
   824	    // and the unlimited chain happily reported "24 images this cycle" while
   825	    // producing ZERO files.
   826	    const renderImage = async (
   827	        i: number,
   828	    ): Promise<{ ok: true; path: string } | { ok: false; reason: string }> => {
   829	        // Neutral: her ART stream, three lanes (Todd 2026-07-29) — the classic
   830	        // still-life deck, free-genre Autocord shoots (any genre/film stock on
   831	        // the TLR), and decoupage tile DESIGNS (flat transferable artwork —
   832	        // "not the tile itself"). All three: NO character injection — Kira's
   833	        // description must not leak into her art. Other modes: tiered Kira pools.
   834	        //
   835	        // Lane + STRUCTURED seed metadata are resolved together (F1 render
   836	        // journal, Inner Loop): F3's taste keys read seed.{stock,style,genre,
   837	        // family}, which the capped intent prose cannot yield back (the stock
   838	        // sits under the ~60-char Autocord suffix; the genre id never appears
   839	        // in the string at all) — so the draw is captured here, at render time.
   840	        const suggested = plan.imageSeeds[i];
   841	        let lane: Exclude<RenderJournalLane, 'video'> = filmStream ? 'still' : 'kira';
   842	        let baseSeed: string;
   843	        let seedMeta: RenderSeedMeta | undefined;
   844	        // Avocado duo film shoots put Kira IN frame (remote release) — those
   845	        // keep the identity stack; all other film-erotic frames render bare.
   846	        let filmShootWithKira = false;
   847	        if (suggested !== undefined) {
   848	            baseSeed = suggested; // a suggestion overrides the lane draw (no structured axes)
   849	        } else if (filmStream) {
   850	            // F3 taste loop (Inner Loop): the rng-lane draws are taste-weighted
   851	            // — Todd's verdicts on past renders bias which style/genre/stock
   852	            // she reaches for. The weigher loads content-taste.json ONCE per
   853	            // draw and never throws (multiplier clamped [0.5, 2.0], decaying
   854	            // toward 1.0, so nothing is starved — review checklist 3/6).
   855	            const weigh = createTasteWeigher();
   856	            const r = Math.random();
   857	            if (r < 0.3) {
   858	                const d = decoupageTileDraw({ weigh });
   859	                lane = 'tile'; baseSeed = d.intent; seedMeta = { style: d.style };
   860	            } else if (r < 0.6) {
   861	                const d = autocordShootDraw({ weigh });
   862	                lane = 'shoot'; baseSeed = d.intent; seedMeta = { genre: d.genre, stock: d.stock };
   863	            } else {
   864	                baseSeed = buildStillLifeIntent();
   865	            }
   866	        } else if ((contentMode === 'banana' || contentMode === 'avocado')
   867	            && hasEroticFilmModels()   // tab-emptied roster turns the lane off
   868	            && Math.random() < EROTIC_FILM_SHARE) {
   869	            // Erotic film photography (Todd 2026-07-29): her barkada as adult
   870	            // models — avant-garde and bold. Banana = suggestive, Kira behind
   871	            // the camera only. Avocado adds explicit studies AND duo frames
   872	            // where Kira steps in by remote release (withKira) — those MUST
   873	            // keep the identity stack + injection so she reads as herself;
   874	            // friend-only frames render bare (the identity LoRA would put HER
   875	            // face on a model).
   876	            const d = contentMode === 'avocado'
   877	                ? eroticFilmDrawAvocado({ weigh: createTasteWeigher() })
   878	                : eroticFilmDraw({ weigh: createTasteWeigher() });
   879	            lane = 'film-erotic'; baseSeed = d.intent;
   880	            filmShootWithKira = d.withKira;
   881	            seedMeta = { stock: d.stock, family: d.family };
   882	        } else {
   883	            const d = buildImageIntentDraw(i, arcState, contentMode);
   884	            baseSeed = d.intent;
   885	            if (d.family) seedMeta = { family: d.family };
   886	        }
   887	        const intent = themedSeed(baseSeed, plan.theme);
   888	        // Tile designs are flat artwork — square, but NO camera shot template
   889	        // (portrait-medium's "medium shot, waist up" framing fights a pattern).
   890	        // Erotic film shoots use the film composition (Autocord 6x6 square).
   891	        const comp = lane === 'tile'
   892	            ? { width: 1024, height: 1024, shot_template: '' }
   893	            : (filmStream || lane === 'film-erotic')
   894	                ? buildComposition(i, { ...arcState, cameraAesthetic: 'film' })
   895	                : buildComposition(i, arcState);
   896	        console.log(`[kira-content-scheduler] Image ${i + 1} intent: ${intent} [${comp.width}x${comp.height} ${comp.shot_template}]`);
   897	        // Sealed orchestrator: local model authors the full explicit prompt
   898	        // (Claude never sees/authors explicit content in this path).
   899	        let r: unknown;
   900	        try {
   901	            r = await ctx.executor.executeLocal('generate_image', {
   902	                prompt: intent,
   903	                character: 'kira',
   904	                content_mode: contentMode,
   905	                // Her art of OTHERS never injects Kira's description — she is
   906	                // the camera. Exception: avocado duo film shoots (withKira)
   907	                // put her in frame by remote release, so injection stays ON.
   908	                // Injection is the ONLY Kira-identity mechanism: the krea-kira
   909	                // stack's LoRAs (KNP + SEAsian + anatomy) are aesthetic/ethnic,
   910	                // not a face LoRA — so models render on the FULL stack (Todd
   911	                // 2026-07-29 "they can all use the Krea-Kira stack considering"
   912	                // their ethnicity); no preset override for the film lane.
   913	                skip_character_injection: filmStream || (lane === 'film-erotic' && !filmShootWithKira),
   914	                // Neutral art stream renders on bare Krea2 — no LoRAs (Todd
   915	                // 2026-07-27 "Neutral can use Krea2 without loras"). Apple
   916	                // renders on the identity-only stack (Todd 2026-07-28 tier
   917	                // hygiene). Film-erotic: mode-default stack, no override.
   918	                ...(filmStream ? { preset: NEUTRAL_ART_PRESET }
   919	                    : contentMode === 'apple' ? { preset: APPLE_SFW_PRESET } : {}),
   920	                width: comp.width,
   921	                height: comp.height,
   922	                shot_template: comp.shot_template,
   923	            });
   924	        } catch (err) {
   925	            return { ok: false, reason: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) };
   926	        }
   927	        // executeLocal wraps the tool return under .data (tool-executor.ts:390);
   928	        // image_path/local_path (NOT `path`) live there.
   929	        const rr = (r ?? {}) as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
   930	        const d = (rr.data ?? rr) as Record<string, unknown>;
   931	        const outPath = (typeof d.image_path === 'string' && d.image_path)
   932	            || (typeof d.local_path === 'string' && d.local_path)
   933	            || '';
   934	        if (!outPath) {
   935	            return { ok: false, reason: String((d.error ?? rr.error) ?? 'no output path in render result') };
   936	        }
   937	        // F1 render journal (Inner Loop): one line per VERIFIED render so her
   938	        // persona knows the work. LOCAL try/catch on top of appendRenderRecord's
   939	        // own never-throw: this runs inside enqueueRender job callbacks, where a
   940	        // throw after result.ok would be swallowed by render-queue containment
   941	        // while the unlimited chain's chainNext never runs — silently killing
   942	        // the rest of the cycle (plan F1 wiring / review checklist 3).
   943	        try {
   944	            appendRenderRecord({
   945	                ts: new Date().toISOString(),
   946	                tier: contentMode,
   947	                lane,
   948	                intent,
   949	                ...(plan.theme ? { theme: plan.theme } : {}),
   950	                path: outPath,
   951	                kind: 'image',
   952	                ...(seedMeta ? { seed: seedMeta } : {}),
   953	            });
   954	        } catch { /* journal is best-effort — never costs a render */ }
   955	        return { ok: true, path: outPath };
   956	    };
   957	
   958	    if (unlimitedImages) {
   959	        // Unlimited-within-cycle (Todd 2026-07-20): chain image renders —
   960	        // each job enqueues the next only while the cycle window is open, so
   961	        // throughput adapts to real render times ("based on logistics") and
   962	        // the queue never carries into the next cycle (no logjams). The
   963	        // 2-minute margin leaves room for the in-flight render to finish;
   964	        // MAX_CHAIN is a runaway backstop, not a target.
   965	        //
   966	        // Failure accounting (2026-07-25): only verified outputs count as
   967	        // images; failures are counted separately and 3 CONSECUTIVE failures
   968	        // break the chain for the rest of the cycle — a dead backend gets 3
   969	        // probes, not 24 pointless retries.
   970	        const MAX_CHAIN = 24;
   971	        const MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES = 3;
   972	        // The chain stops at whichever closes first: the cycle budget or the
   973	        // content window (so an 8am edge actually ends an overnight chain
   974	        // instead of the final cycle running long past it).
   975	        const windowCloseMs = msUntilWindowClose(new Date(), activeHours);
   976	        // Floor at 0 (Codex W11): a pathologically short interval yields
   977	        // exactly one render per cycle, never a negative-budget queue.
   978	        const cycleBudgetMs = Math.max(0, intervalMinutes * 60_000 - 120_000);
   979	        const cycleEndsAt = Date.now()
   980	            + (windowCloseMs === null ? cycleBudgetMs : Math.min(cycleBudgetMs, windowCloseMs));
   981	        let images = 0;
   982	        let failures = 0;
   983	        let consecutiveFailures = 0;
   984	        const logChainDone = (): void => {
   985	            console.log(`[kira-content-scheduler] Unlimited chain done: ${images} images, ${failures} failures this cycle`);
   986	        };
   987	        const chainNext = (i: number): void => {
   988	            // Autonomous 24/7 content is the priority FLOOR (Todd): 'low' so any
   989	            // work / Bree / studio / gallery request preempts the backlog.
   990	            enqueueRender('image', async () => {
   991	                const result = await renderImage(i);
   992	                if (result.ok) {
   993	                    images++;
   994	                    consecutiveFailures = 0;
   995	                } else {
   996	                    failures++;
   997	                    consecutiveFailures++;
   998	                    console.warn(`[kira-content-scheduler] render FAILED (${result.reason}) — ${consecutiveFailures} consecutive failures`);
   999	                    if (consecutiveFailures >= MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES) {
  1000	                        console.warn(`[kira-content-scheduler] Unlimited chain BROKEN after ${consecutiveFailures} consecutive failures — skipping the rest of this cycle`);
  1001	                        logChainDone();
  1002	                        return;
  1003	                    }
  1004	                }
  1005	                if (Date.now() < cycleEndsAt && i + 1 < MAX_CHAIN) {
  1006	                    chainNext(i + 1);
  1007	                } else {
  1008	                    logChainDone();
  1009	                }
  1010	            }, { priority: 'low' });
  1011	        };
  1012	        chainNext(0);
  1013	    } else {
  1014	        for (let i = 0; i < imageCount; i++) {
  1015	            // 24/7 content is the priority floor — see chainNext above.
  1016	            enqueueRender('image', async () => {
  1017	                const result = await renderImage(i);
  1018	                if (!result.ok) {
  1019	                    console.warn(`[kira-content-scheduler] Image ${i + 1} render FAILED (${result.reason})`);
  1020	                }
  1021	            }, { priority: 'low' });
  1022	        }
  1023	    }
  1024	
  1025	    // Videos: videoMode-driven (t2v UN-pinned, Todd 2026-07-28 "mix it up" —
  1026	    // supersedes the 2026-07-20 i2v-only pin). Per video, pickVideoMode
  1027	    // resolves 'i2v' | 't2v' ('mixed' coin-flips):
  1028	    //   i2v — the anchored path: seed frame (pose-locking control map when the
  1029	    //         action has one), VLM adequacy check, then animate;
  1030	    //   t2v — the whole clip from a buildT2VScene prompt (no seed, 720p — the
  1031	    //         validated LTX recipe, docs/methods/nsfw-ltx-t2v-recipe.md).
  1032	    if (filmStream && videoCount > 0) {
  1033	        console.log('[kira-content-scheduler] neutral (film) cycle — skipping videos (Autocord stills only)');
  1034	    }

  1040

  1042	            const scenePrompt = buildT2VScene(contentMode);

  1044	                console.log(`[kira-content-scheduler] Video ${i + 1} t2v scene: ${scenePrompt.slice(0, 120)}… (${aspectRatio}, ${clipSeconds}s)`);

  1046	                    prompt: scenePrompt,
  1047	                    character: 'kira',
  1048	                    content_mode: contentMode,
  1049	                    aspect_ratio: aspectRatio,
  1050	                    resolution: '720p', // t2v has no 480p tier
  1051	                    duration: clipSeconds,
  1052	                });

  1069	                    });
  1070	                } catch { /* best-effort */ }
  1071	            }, { priority: 'low' });
  1072	            continue;
  1073	        }

  1076	        // frame) and carries the matched action + any pose-locking control map.
  1077	        const concept = buildVideoConcept(contentMode);

  1079	        // matched action + control stay the concept's — i2v still needs a motion

  1622	
  1623	/**
  1624	 * Video mode for the 24/7 cycle — t2v UN-pinned (Todd 2026-07-28 "unpin t2v /
  1625	 * mix it up", superseding the 2026-07-20 i2v-only pin). Honors the configured
  1626	 * videoMode: 'i2v' | 't2v' pass through; 'mixed' coin-flips per video (rng
  1627	 * injectable for tests); anything else — including absent config — falls back
  1628	 * to 'i2v' so un-pinning never changes behavior for configs that didn't ask.
  1629	 */
  1630	/** Clamp policy clipSeconds to the proven single-pass window (4-12s; 12s =
  1631	 * 289f — beyond that is chunked continuation with seam risk). Frames derive
  1632	 * downstream: duration x 24fps snapped to the 1+8k grid. */
  1633	export function resolveClipSeconds(raw?: number): number {
  1634	    if (raw === undefined || raw === null || !Number.isFinite(raw)) return I2V_CLIP_SECONDS;
  1635	    return Math.min(CLIP_SECONDS_MAX, Math.max(CLIP_SECONDS_MIN, Math.round(raw)));
  1636	}
  1637	
  1638	export function pickVideoMode(mode: string, _index: number, rng: () => number = Math.random): 'i2v' | 't2v' {
  1639	    if (mode === 't2v') return 't2v';
  1640	    if (mode === 'mixed') return rng() < 0.5 ? 'i2v' : 't2v';
  1641	    return 'i2v';
  1642	}

  1644	/**
  1645	 * Build a full-scene T2V prompt (text→video). Unlike i2v (which animates a base
  1646	 * frame), t2v generates the whole clip from text, so the prompt is a detailed
  1647	 * cinematic scene — subject + setting + action + camera + mood. Identity is
  1648	 * anchored by the kira-video-<mode> preset LoRA, so Kira stays Kira without a
  1649	 * base frame. Detailed/longer prompts render better on LTX-2 t2v.
  1650	 */
