## Verdict

Do not implement this draft yet. The reservation-ledger direction is sound, but several stated guarantees are false against the current stack. Most critically, the lease protocol cannot be implemented through the coordinator actor, engine-front insertion is neither atomic nor addressable by the submitted job ID, and the 10-minute responsiveness claim is incompatible with current production video runtimes.

Findings below are ordered by recommended fold-in priority.

### 1. [BLOCKER] The lease endpoint cannot use `WarmServerCoordinator`

A step-boundary pause is technically possible for LTX: each iteration calls synchronous `eval(currentLatents)` before invoking the progress callback, so previously submitted Metal work is complete at that boundary ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:1146](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1146), [LTX2Pipeline.swift:1224](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1224), [mlx transforms.cpp:304](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/.build/checkouts/mlx-swift/Source/Cmlx/mlx/mlx/transforms.cpp:304)).

But local video executes a synchronous closure inside the coordinator actor for the full render ([WarmServer.swift:5337](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5337), [WarmServer.swift:5407](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5407)). The server already had to move health/queue reads to lock-backed snapshots because actor calls block behind that render ([WarmServer.swift:2313](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2313)).

Therefore `POST /v1/gpu/lease` implemented as a coordinator method will wait until the render finishes—the opposite of the intended behavior.

Fold into the spec:

- A lock/condition-backed `GPUQuiescenceGate`, independent of the actor.
- Distinct `requested` and `granted` states. A lease is not usable until the render has actually reached a boundary.
- TTL begins at grant, not request.
- The LTX hook runs immediately after `eval`, before the next transformer call.
- Holder token, monotonic expiry, idempotent release, shutdown wake-up.
- A dedicated boundary hook rather than overloading the telemetry callback.
- Equivalent hooks must be wired into other pipelines; WarmServer explicitly says non-Flux1 families currently lack server-level step callbacks ([WarmServer.swift:5423](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5423)).

### 2. [BLOCKER] “Text answers ≤ one denoise step” is false during VAE and setup phases

LTX has no callback during:

- Text encoding.
- I2V VAE encoding.
- Model loading/admission.
- Latent upsampling.
- VAE decode and postprocessing.

Decode follows denoising directly without a boundary hook ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:644](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:644)). The code documents a measured ~25 GB decode intermediate and silent corruption under pressure ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:1268](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1268)). A lease requested after the last denoise callback may wait through the entire decode; if the API returns “granted” before quiescence, inference instead overlaps the riskiest memory phase.

Pausing also leaves the 40–65 GB LTX model and live latents resident. `HeavyModelAdmission` only models image versus video residency ([HeavyModelAdmission.swift:18](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/HeavyModelAdmission.swift:18), [HeavyModelAdmission.swift:35](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/HeavyModelAdmission.swift:35)); it provides no admission decision for a VLM or another large inference model.

Replace the blanket latency guarantee with a phase-aware contract:

- `denoising`: grant at next evaluated step boundary.
- `encode/load/refine-transition/decode`: defer or reject with `retry_after`.
- `quiesced`: inference may start only after memory admission succeeds.
- Every inference consumer declares expected resident and transient memory, not merely `class: inference`.
- Publish current phase and `max_uninterruptible_sec`.

### 3. [BLOCKER] Lease ownership can self-deadlock around prompt optimization

Prompt optimization currently runs outside the engine FIFO both through `/v1/enhance` ([WarmServer.swift:2368](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2368)) and implicitly during `prepareLocalVideo`, before engine enqueue ([WarmServer.swift:2117](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2117)).

A naïve “every inference call takes a lease” implementation can deadlock when:

1. The daemon holds a lease.
2. It submits a render.
3. The render endpoint’s implicit optimizer tries to acquire another lease.
4. The original holder waits for submission to return.

The current daemon deliberately prevents double optimization by sending `enhance:false` after its own optimizer pass ([video-tools.ts:1081](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:1081)).

Specify:

- Lease tokens are propagated and reentrant only for the same holder.
- Batch optimization happens before render submission.
- Batched renders must carry `enhance:false`.
- No queued render may synchronously acquire an inference lease from inside its execution entry.
- Fairness is measured in paused seconds plus a minimum render-run quantum. “Maximum N leases” alone allows one 90-second lease to be much worse than several short leases.

### 4. [BLOCKER] Engine-front bump is racy and uses the wrong job ID

There are two queues:

1. The daemon’s in-process priority queue, where interactive work is already `high` and scheduled content is `low` ([render-queue.ts:31](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/media-pool/render-queue.ts:31), [async-envelope.ts:390](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/async-envelope.ts:390), [content-scheduler.ts:988](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:988)).
2. WarmServer’s append-only FIFO ([WarmServer.swift:5047](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5047)).

The proposed “enqueue, then reorder to front” is not atomic. If the engine is idle, processing may begin immediately before the move request arrives. Worse, `VideoJobTracker` creates a public polling ID ([WarmServer.swift:4278](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:4278)), while the coordinator creates a different queue ID ([WarmServer.swift:4453](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:4453)). The returned video `job_id` cannot identify the row accepted by `/v1/queue/{id}/move`.

The local daemon queue also holds composite callbacks through completion, so most scheduled work has not yet been submitted to the engine. Engine reorder cannot bump ahead of work still sitting in that upstream queue.

Required correction:

- Carry priority atomically in every engine submit: `enqueue(... priority:, reservationId:, jobId:)`.
- Use one externally supplied job ID from reservation through daemon, engine queue, tracker, trace, and gallery.
- Preserve stable FIFO within priority.
- Either consolidate the two queues or expose both and define which layer owns prioritization.
- Do not describe the existing reorder endpoint as the bump mechanism.

### 5. [BLOCKER] The 10-minute contiguous-occupancy guarantee is unsatisfiable

The spec says a running job is never killed while promising media wait is bounded by the 10-minute occupancy cap ([kira-reservation-scheduler.md:77](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/specs/kira-reservation-scheduler.md:77), [kira-reservation-scheduler.md:102](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/specs/kira-reservation-scheduler.md:102)).

Current evidence contradicts the proposed 5–6-minute video estimate:

- Banana/avocado I2V has a 241-frame minimum ([video-tools.ts:275](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:275)).
- The deadline code records a 97-frame two-stage render measured at 923 seconds—over 15 minutes ([video-tools.ts:243](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:243)).
- The old 10-minute tool cap no longer exists: the outer timeout is now 160 minutes, with a 25-minute base poll budget ([video-tools.ts:234](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:234)).

Priority does nothing to shorten the active job. Inference leases help text cognition during denoising, but do not allow an interactive media render to run concurrently.

The spec must choose one:

- Long scheduled videos run only in explicitly non-interactive windows.
- The cap becomes a conservative admission rule: never start a scheduled job whose P95 runtime plus setup exceeds the remaining occupancy quantum.
- Or the media-response SLA admits that the current job may exceed 10 minutes.

A median estimate is not adequate for this admission decision.

### 6. [BLOCKER] FPS is not available end-to-end on Kira’s actual path

WarmServer REST accepts `fps`, but Kira submits through the MCP `generate_video` tool. That tool exposes neither playback `fps` nor `frame_rate` ([MCPToolRegistry.swift:421](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/MCP/MCPToolRegistry.swift:421)), and its executor does not forward either field ([MCPToolExecutor.swift:512](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/MCP/MCPToolExecutor.swift:512)). The daemon nevertheless sends `frame_rate`, so it is currently discarded ([video-tools.ts:1097](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:1097)).

There are also three conflicting time quantities:

- Playback `fps`.
- `cond_fps`, the temporal-RoPE motion dial ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:1450](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1450)).
- Frame count, which must be `1 + 8k`.

Current I2V frame selection uses a hardcoded conditioning FPS of 24 and may force 241 frames regardless of requested clip duration ([video-tools.ts:287](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:287)). At playback 16 fps, 241 frames is about 15 seconds, not an 8-second clip.

Additionally, WarmServer only derives a new frame count when `duration` exceeds the default chunk’s duration ([WarmServer.swift:1819](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:1819), [WarmServer.swift:2163](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2163)). A 4-second/16-fps request can therefore remain 97 frames and play for roughly 6 seconds.

Make the contract explicit:

```text
targetFrames = snapUpTo1Plus8K(clipSeconds * playbackFps)
require targetFrames <= 289
condFps affects RoPE only
actualClipSeconds = targetFrames / playbackFps
```

The 12-second ceiling is valid only around 24 fps. At 30 fps, a 12-second single-pass clip exceeds 289 frames. Validate the `(clipSeconds, fps)` pair, not each field independently, and send explicit `frames`, `fps`, and `tuning.condFps`.

### 7. [BLOCKER] The current policy API will lose scheduler data during migration

`clipSeconds` is checked but never assigned to `next.clipSeconds` ([kira-api.ts:342](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:342)). The writer also omits it entirely ([kira-api.ts:453](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:453)). Consequently, the recently shipped PUT surface cannot persist a clip-duration edit.

The writer reconstructs the whole `contentScheduler` block and preserves only `arcLifecycle`. If MediaTypes, slot templates, estimates, or ledger metadata are stored there, an old Kira-tab save will erase them. The desktop client also does not parse `clipSeconds` today ([KiraClient.swift:120](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ComfyBoxDesktop/Kira/KiraClient.swift:120)).

There is another behavior-changing migration trap: the API reports absent `videoMode` as `mixed` ([kira-api.ts:283](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:283)), while the running scheduler treats absence as `i2v` ([content-scheduler.ts:661](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:661)). Reading and resaving policy can therefore unpin T2V unintentionally.

Before P1:

- Fix `clipSeconds` assignment/persistence and add tests.
- Preserve unknown/new fields or use a separate versioned `/v1/kira/schedule/config` document.
- Add `schemaVersion` and optimistic concurrency/ETag.
- Define one authoritative default for `videoMode`.
- Keep old clients read-compatible without allowing them to overwrite new fields.

### 8. [HIGH] `Σ estCostSec` is not a valid cost model for this engine

Costs are strongly sequence-dependent:

- Starting video evicts all image models, drains memory for up to roughly 18 seconds, then runs admission ([WarmServer.swift:5342](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5342)).
- The next image reloads the previous image model ([WarmServer.swift:5467](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5467)).
- An i2v daemon job can include seed image generation, up to three VLM QA attempts, the memory gate, and video generation ([content-scheduler.ts:1092](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1092), [content-scheduler.ts:1131](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1131)).
- Warm versus cold back-to-back videos have different admission requirements.

A rolling median per MediaType cannot “prove” fit. It suppresses the tail that matters, lags configuration changes, ignores cold/warm state, and may exclude failures or admission refusals.

Use:

- A normalized execution signature: type, dimensions, frames, steps, two-stage, decoder mode, source mode, resident model state.
- P90/P95 or median plus robust deviation for admission; median can remain the UI estimate.
- Explicit transition/setup costs between model families.
- Minimum samples and a conservative configured floor.
- Estimate-version reset when recipe parameters change.
- Separate measurements for queue delay, optimizer/VLM time, model transition, denoise, decode, and delivery.

Also split two accounting concepts currently conflated by the spec: interactive work may not debit the content quota, but it absolutely reduces remaining wall-clock capacity and must force a re-fit.

### 9. [HIGH] Unlimited mode and backlog rules can create permanent overload

Current unlimited mode is an online filler loop: it submits one image, waits for the actual result, and only then decides whether to chain another ([content-scheduler.ts:958](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:958)). It is not a finite set of reservations. It also starts at least one job even when the computed cycle budget is zero, and its time check occurs only after completion ([content-scheduler.ts:976](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:976), [content-scheduler.ts:1005](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1005)).

Translate `unlimitedImages` into a lowest-priority filler policy, not an infinite slot template:

```text
fillRemainingWith: image.<tier>
start only when conservativeCost <= timeUntilNextProtectedWindow
```

The backlog policy also needs to be resolved before P1, not left as an open question ([kira-reservation-scheduler.md:195](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/specs/kira-reservation-scheduler.md:195)). “Push to next slot’s front” causes:

- Permanent head-of-line blocking for a reservation larger than slot capacity.
- Unbounded growth when template offered load exceeds service capacity.
- Starvation of new slot content by old backlog.
- Scheduled starvation under sustained interactive traffic.

Specify maximum age/slots, maximum attempts, oversized quarantine, bounded backlog, aging/deficit fairness, and overload telemetry.

### 10. [HIGH] Reservation type must be resolved at planning time

Today `videoMode: mixed` coin-flips i2v versus t2v while executing the event ([content-scheduler.ts:1025](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1025), [content-scheduler.ts:1638](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1638)). That is too late for a ledger whose row, estimated cost, engine recipe, and QA aggregation are type-specific.

Persist the concrete choice at planning time, including:

- MediaType ID and immutable recipe revision.
- Concrete i2v/t2v mode.
- Duration, playback FPS, frame count, dimensions and tuning.
- Seed/template/suggestion linkage.
- Reservation ID and idempotency key.

Neutral/film must retain its hard “stills only” rule ([content-scheduler.ts:730](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:730)). Tier rotation and Todd/Kira overrides must also be represented explicitly rather than silently replaced by slot templates.

The priority definition needs a trigger distinction: a suggestion-box idea consumed by a scheduled cycle is still scheduled—the current scheduler consumes those ideas as part of its scheduled plan ([content-scheduler.ts:782](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:782)). Calling every Muse suggestion-box pick `INTERACTIVE` would let the scheduled planner relabel its own work and starve the schedule.

### 11. [HIGH] Two classes conflate priority, GPU behavior, and job shape

`INTERACTIVE/SCHEDULED` is a priority axis. `render/inference` is a resource-behavior axis. They should not be the same model.

Current job kinds demonstrate why:

- SeedVR2/ESRGAN upscale runs synchronously outside the coordinator queue ([WarmServer.swift:2942](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2942), [WarmServer.swift:3077](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:3077)). It is GPU work but not step-pausable.
- Montage is synchronous, memory-light editorial work and should not consume diffusion capacity ([WarmServer.swift:1061](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:1061)).
- Storyboard is a composite parent whose image/video child jobs deliberately take separate FIFO turns ([WarmServer.swift:4329](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:4329), [WarmServer.swift:3476](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:3476)).
- Face swap is a desktop-only external Python process; the server never sees it ([FaceSwapService.swift:1](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ComfyBoxDesktop/Face/FaceSwapService.swift:1)).

Model at least:

```text
priority: interactive | userBackground | scheduled
resource: gpuDiffusion | gpuNonPausable | gpuInference | cpuMedia | external
preemption: stepBoundary | phaseBoundary | none
shape: atomic | composite
memoryEstimate / runtimeEstimate
```

All GPU paths, especially upscale, must join the same admission broker before the lease guarantee can claim “ANY model sharing the GPU.” Face swap cannot be reserved or centrally displayed until it is brokered by the server.

### 12. [HIGH] A persisted ledger does not provide exactly-once execution

The daemon render queue is explicitly in-memory and loses pending jobs on restart ([render-queue.ts:1](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/media-pool/render-queue.ts:1)). WarmServer persistence only recovers `generate` and `lora_swap`; local video, ControlNet, model switches, and composites are not serializable ([QueuePersistence.swift:9](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/QueuePersistence.swift:9)).

A ledger cannot safely infer whether a vanished submission should be retried without a durable, shared identity. Otherwise restart yields either duplicate media or lost reservations.

Add a reservation state machine:

```text
reserved → dispatching → engineQueued → running → succeeded|failed|cancelled|lost
```

Requirements:

- Stable `reservationId` accepted idempotently by the engine.
- The same ID in queue rows, tracker status, trace, gallery metadata and delivery journal.
- Atomic state persistence before and after submission.
- Reconcile by ID, not merely by discovering an output file.
- Explicit retry/at-least-once policy per job kind.

### 13. [HIGH] The proposed P1 cutover can double-run or skip the 24/7 stream

The current scheduler uses a process-relative `setInterval`, with the first tick five seconds after startup ([content-scheduler.ts:503](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:503)). A persisted slot ledger needs stable slot IDs anchored to a defined timezone and epoch; otherwise a restart can create a second “current cycle.”

The pause sentinel only prevents future ticks. It does not retract an already-emitted scheduler event or pending low-priority callbacks. There is also historical evidence of double-started scheduler timers, guarded only by an in-process flag ([content-scheduler.ts:506](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:506)).

Safe migration sequence:

1. Fix the policy/FPS/job-ID contracts.
2. Deploy a shadow planner that persists proposed slots but submits nothing.
3. Compare shadow output with current tick behavior, including rotation, neutral film, mixed-mode resolution and unlimited filler.
4. Fence the old scheduled emitter with a durable scheduler generation/owner ID.
5. Stop only new scheduled emission; keep interactive traffic alive.
6. Drain or reconcile already-emitted daemon and engine work.
7. Activate one ledger slot at the next stable boundary.
8. Keep a rollback switch that resumes the old tick without replaying the same slot.

### 14. [HIGH] The desktop needs four strata, and current APIs cannot supply them

The real execution stack has:

1. Running in engine.
2. Pending in engine.
3. Pending in the daemon’s local priority queue.
4. Reserved in the scheduler ledger.

The draft omits stratum 3, where most scheduled composite callbacks and fast-lane jobs wait before engine submission.

Current `/v1/queue` exposes only ID, kind, summary, source and enqueue time for pending work; the active row does not even include kind ([WarmServer.swift:2320](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2320)). It has no priority, MediaType, reservation ID, estimate, deadline, parent job, or capabilities. The desktop mirrors that limited shape exactly ([EngineService.swift:1270](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ComfyBoxDesktop/EngineService.swift:1270)).

Local video operations already appear in the engine pending list as generic `kind: video` ([WarmServer.swift:5219](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5219)); the draft’s “make video jobs appear” premise is therefore stale. What remains invisible is:

- Video work waiting in the daemon queue.
- Cloud video.
- Storyboard parent state.
- Montage and upscale.
- Desktop-local face swap.
- Reservation metadata.
- Public tracker-to-queue ID correlation.

`kira-api.ts` currently has only status/pause/resume/policy scheduler routes—no ledger endpoint or mutation routes ([kira-api.ts:7](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:7)). A GET ledger alone also cannot implement the promised push/pull/cancel controls.

Define a versioned aggregate DTO or make the desktop explicitly join both services. It needs:

- `reservationId`, `engineJobId`, `daemonJobId`, parent/child IDs.
- `stratum`, priority, resource class, MediaType/revision.
- Planned/start/deadline timestamps and conservative estimate.
- Phase/progress and lease state.
- Per-row allowed actions.
- Versioned mutation endpoints with conflict detection.

## Recommended fold-in sequence

1. Lease/quiescence protocol and phase-aware memory admission: findings 1–3.
2. End-to-end atomic priority and truthful SLA: findings 4–5.
3. Repair FPS and policy contracts before storing new configuration: findings 6–7.
4. Replace additive-median packing with stateful conservative planning: findings 8–10.
5. Generalize resource/job taxonomy and durability: findings 11–12.
6. Specify shadow cutover and only then build the desktop aggregation surface: findings 13–14.

This was a read-only review; no code or tests were changed.
tokens used
293,851
## Verdict

Do not implement this draft yet. The reservation-ledger direction is sound, but several stated guarantees are false against the current stack. Most critically, the lease protocol cannot be implemented through the coordinator actor, engine-front insertion is neither atomic nor addressable by the submitted job ID, and the 10-minute responsiveness claim is incompatible with current production video runtimes.

Findings below are ordered by recommended fold-in priority.

### 1. [BLOCKER] The lease endpoint cannot use `WarmServerCoordinator`

A step-boundary pause is technically possible for LTX: each iteration calls synchronous `eval(currentLatents)` before invoking the progress callback, so previously submitted Metal work is complete at that boundary ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:1146](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1146), [LTX2Pipeline.swift:1224](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1224), [mlx transforms.cpp:304](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/.build/checkouts/mlx-swift/Source/Cmlx/mlx/mlx/transforms.cpp:304)).

But local video executes a synchronous closure inside the coordinator actor for the full render ([WarmServer.swift:5337](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5337), [WarmServer.swift:5407](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5407)). The server already had to move health/queue reads to lock-backed snapshots because actor calls block behind that render ([WarmServer.swift:2313](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2313)).

Therefore `POST /v1/gpu/lease` implemented as a coordinator method will wait until the render finishes—the opposite of the intended behavior.

Fold into the spec:

- A lock/condition-backed `GPUQuiescenceGate`, independent of the actor.
- Distinct `requested` and `granted` states. A lease is not usable until the render has actually reached a boundary.
- TTL begins at grant, not request.
- The LTX hook runs immediately after `eval`, before the next transformer call.
- Holder token, monotonic expiry, idempotent release, shutdown wake-up.
- A dedicated boundary hook rather than overloading the telemetry callback.
- Equivalent hooks must be wired into other pipelines; WarmServer explicitly says non-Flux1 families currently lack server-level step callbacks ([WarmServer.swift:5423](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5423)).

### 2. [BLOCKER] “Text answers ≤ one denoise step” is false during VAE and setup phases

LTX has no callback during:

- Text encoding.
- I2V VAE encoding.
- Model loading/admission.
- Latent upsampling.
- VAE decode and postprocessing.

Decode follows denoising directly without a boundary hook ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:644](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:644)). The code documents a measured ~25 GB decode intermediate and silent corruption under pressure ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:1268](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1268)). A lease requested after the last denoise callback may wait through the entire decode; if the API returns “granted” before quiescence, inference instead overlaps the riskiest memory phase.

Pausing also leaves the 40–65 GB LTX model and live latents resident. `HeavyModelAdmission` only models image versus video residency ([HeavyModelAdmission.swift:18](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/HeavyModelAdmission.swift:18), [HeavyModelAdmission.swift:35](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/HeavyModelAdmission.swift:35)); it provides no admission decision for a VLM or another large inference model.

Replace the blanket latency guarantee with a phase-aware contract:

- `denoising`: grant at next evaluated step boundary.
- `encode/load/refine-transition/decode`: defer or reject with `retry_after`.
- `quiesced`: inference may start only after memory admission succeeds.
- Every inference consumer declares expected resident and transient memory, not merely `class: inference`.
- Publish current phase and `max_uninterruptible_sec`.

### 3. [BLOCKER] Lease ownership can self-deadlock around prompt optimization

Prompt optimization currently runs outside the engine FIFO both through `/v1/enhance` ([WarmServer.swift:2368](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2368)) and implicitly during `prepareLocalVideo`, before engine enqueue ([WarmServer.swift:2117](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2117)).

A naïve “every inference call takes a lease” implementation can deadlock when:

1. The daemon holds a lease.
2. It submits a render.
3. The render endpoint’s implicit optimizer tries to acquire another lease.
4. The original holder waits for submission to return.

The current daemon deliberately prevents double optimization by sending `enhance:false` after its own optimizer pass ([video-tools.ts:1081](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:1081)).

Specify:

- Lease tokens are propagated and reentrant only for the same holder.
- Batch optimization happens before render submission.
- Batched renders must carry `enhance:false`.
- No queued render may synchronously acquire an inference lease from inside its execution entry.
- Fairness is measured in paused seconds plus a minimum render-run quantum. “Maximum N leases” alone allows one 90-second lease to be much worse than several short leases.

### 4. [BLOCKER] Engine-front bump is racy and uses the wrong job ID

There are two queues:

1. The daemon’s in-process priority queue, where interactive work is already `high` and scheduled content is `low` ([render-queue.ts:31](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/media-pool/render-queue.ts:31), [async-envelope.ts:390](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/async-envelope.ts:390), [content-scheduler.ts:988](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:988)).
2. WarmServer’s append-only FIFO ([WarmServer.swift:5047](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5047)).

The proposed “enqueue, then reorder to front” is not atomic. If the engine is idle, processing may begin immediately before the move request arrives. Worse, `VideoJobTracker` creates a public polling ID ([WarmServer.swift:4278](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:4278)), while the coordinator creates a different queue ID ([WarmServer.swift:4453](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:4453)). The returned video `job_id` cannot identify the row accepted by `/v1/queue/{id}/move`.

The local daemon queue also holds composite callbacks through completion, so most scheduled work has not yet been submitted to the engine. Engine reorder cannot bump ahead of work still sitting in that upstream queue.

Required correction:

- Carry priority atomically in every engine submit: `enqueue(... priority:, reservationId:, jobId:)`.
- Use one externally supplied job ID from reservation through daemon, engine queue, tracker, trace, and gallery.
- Preserve stable FIFO within priority.
- Either consolidate the two queues or expose both and define which layer owns prioritization.
- Do not describe the existing reorder endpoint as the bump mechanism.

### 5. [BLOCKER] The 10-minute contiguous-occupancy guarantee is unsatisfiable

The spec says a running job is never killed while promising media wait is bounded by the 10-minute occupancy cap ([kira-reservation-scheduler.md:77](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/specs/kira-reservation-scheduler.md:77), [kira-reservation-scheduler.md:102](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/specs/kira-reservation-scheduler.md:102)).

Current evidence contradicts the proposed 5–6-minute video estimate:

- Banana/avocado I2V has a 241-frame minimum ([video-tools.ts:275](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:275)).
- The deadline code records a 97-frame two-stage render measured at 923 seconds—over 15 minutes ([video-tools.ts:243](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:243)).
- The old 10-minute tool cap no longer exists: the outer timeout is now 160 minutes, with a 25-minute base poll budget ([video-tools.ts:234](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:234)).

Priority does nothing to shorten the active job. Inference leases help text cognition during denoising, but do not allow an interactive media render to run concurrently.

The spec must choose one:

- Long scheduled videos run only in explicitly non-interactive windows.
- The cap becomes a conservative admission rule: never start a scheduled job whose P95 runtime plus setup exceeds the remaining occupancy quantum.
- Or the media-response SLA admits that the current job may exceed 10 minutes.

A median estimate is not adequate for this admission decision.

### 6. [BLOCKER] FPS is not available end-to-end on Kira’s actual path

WarmServer REST accepts `fps`, but Kira submits through the MCP `generate_video` tool. That tool exposes neither playback `fps` nor `frame_rate` ([MCPToolRegistry.swift:421](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/MCP/MCPToolRegistry.swift:421)), and its executor does not forward either field ([MCPToolExecutor.swift:512](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/MCP/MCPToolExecutor.swift:512)). The daemon nevertheless sends `frame_rate`, so it is currently discarded ([video-tools.ts:1097](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:1097)).

There are also three conflicting time quantities:

- Playback `fps`.
- `cond_fps`, the temporal-RoPE motion dial ([LTX2Pipeline.swift:1450](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/LTX2/LTX2Pipeline.swift:1450)).
- Frame count, which must be `1 + 8k`.

Current I2V frame selection uses a hardcoded conditioning FPS of 24 and may force 241 frames regardless of requested clip duration ([video-tools.ts:287](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/tools/video-tools.ts:287)). At playback 16 fps, 241 frames is about 15 seconds, not an 8-second clip.

Additionally, WarmServer only derives a new frame count when `duration` exceeds the default chunk’s duration ([WarmServer.swift:1819](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:1819), [WarmServer.swift:2163](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2163)). A 4-second/16-fps request can therefore remain 97 frames and play for roughly 6 seconds.

Make the contract explicit:

```text
targetFrames = snapUpTo1Plus8K(clipSeconds * playbackFps)
require targetFrames <= 289
condFps affects RoPE only
actualClipSeconds = targetFrames / playbackFps
```

The 12-second ceiling is valid only around 24 fps. At 30 fps, a 12-second single-pass clip exceeds 289 frames. Validate the `(clipSeconds, fps)` pair, not each field independently, and send explicit `frames`, `fps`, and `tuning.condFps`.

### 7. [BLOCKER] The current policy API will lose scheduler data during migration

`clipSeconds` is checked but never assigned to `next.clipSeconds` ([kira-api.ts:342](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:342)). The writer also omits it entirely ([kira-api.ts:453](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:453)). Consequently, the recently shipped PUT surface cannot persist a clip-duration edit.

The writer reconstructs the whole `contentScheduler` block and preserves only `arcLifecycle`. If MediaTypes, slot templates, estimates, or ledger metadata are stored there, an old Kira-tab save will erase them. The desktop client also does not parse `clipSeconds` today ([KiraClient.swift:120](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ComfyBoxDesktop/Kira/KiraClient.swift:120)).

There is another behavior-changing migration trap: the API reports absent `videoMode` as `mixed` ([kira-api.ts:283](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:283)), while the running scheduler treats absence as `i2v` ([content-scheduler.ts:661](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:661)). Reading and resaving policy can therefore unpin T2V unintentionally.

Before P1:

- Fix `clipSeconds` assignment/persistence and add tests.
- Preserve unknown/new fields or use a separate versioned `/v1/kira/schedule/config` document.
- Add `schemaVersion` and optimistic concurrency/ETag.
- Define one authoritative default for `videoMode`.
- Keep old clients read-compatible without allowing them to overwrite new fields.

### 8. [HIGH] `Σ estCostSec` is not a valid cost model for this engine

Costs are strongly sequence-dependent:

- Starting video evicts all image models, drains memory for up to roughly 18 seconds, then runs admission ([WarmServer.swift:5342](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5342)).
- The next image reloads the previous image model ([WarmServer.swift:5467](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5467)).
- An i2v daemon job can include seed image generation, up to three VLM QA attempts, the memory gate, and video generation ([content-scheduler.ts:1092](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1092), [content-scheduler.ts:1131](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1131)).
- Warm versus cold back-to-back videos have different admission requirements.

A rolling median per MediaType cannot “prove” fit. It suppresses the tail that matters, lags configuration changes, ignores cold/warm state, and may exclude failures or admission refusals.

Use:

- A normalized execution signature: type, dimensions, frames, steps, two-stage, decoder mode, source mode, resident model state.
- P90/P95 or median plus robust deviation for admission; median can remain the UI estimate.
- Explicit transition/setup costs between model families.
- Minimum samples and a conservative configured floor.
- Estimate-version reset when recipe parameters change.
- Separate measurements for queue delay, optimizer/VLM time, model transition, denoise, decode, and delivery.

Also split two accounting concepts currently conflated by the spec: interactive work may not debit the content quota, but it absolutely reduces remaining wall-clock capacity and must force a re-fit.

### 9. [HIGH] Unlimited mode and backlog rules can create permanent overload

Current unlimited mode is an online filler loop: it submits one image, waits for the actual result, and only then decides whether to chain another ([content-scheduler.ts:958](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:958)). It is not a finite set of reservations. It also starts at least one job even when the computed cycle budget is zero, and its time check occurs only after completion ([content-scheduler.ts:976](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:976), [content-scheduler.ts:1005](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1005)).

Translate `unlimitedImages` into a lowest-priority filler policy, not an infinite slot template:

```text
fillRemainingWith: image.<tier>
start only when conservativeCost <= timeUntilNextProtectedWindow
```

The backlog policy also needs to be resolved before P1, not left as an open question ([kira-reservation-scheduler.md:195](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/specs/kira-reservation-scheduler.md:195)). “Push to next slot’s front” causes:

- Permanent head-of-line blocking for a reservation larger than slot capacity.
- Unbounded growth when template offered load exceeds service capacity.
- Starvation of new slot content by old backlog.
- Scheduled starvation under sustained interactive traffic.

Specify maximum age/slots, maximum attempts, oversized quarantine, bounded backlog, aging/deficit fairness, and overload telemetry.

### 10. [HIGH] Reservation type must be resolved at planning time

Today `videoMode: mixed` coin-flips i2v versus t2v while executing the event ([content-scheduler.ts:1025](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1025), [content-scheduler.ts:1638](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:1638)). That is too late for a ledger whose row, estimated cost, engine recipe, and QA aggregation are type-specific.

Persist the concrete choice at planning time, including:

- MediaType ID and immutable recipe revision.
- Concrete i2v/t2v mode.
- Duration, playback FPS, frame count, dimensions and tuning.
- Seed/template/suggestion linkage.
- Reservation ID and idempotency key.

Neutral/film must retain its hard “stills only” rule ([content-scheduler.ts:730](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:730)). Tier rotation and Todd/Kira overrides must also be represented explicitly rather than silently replaced by slot templates.

The priority definition needs a trigger distinction: a suggestion-box idea consumed by a scheduled cycle is still scheduled—the current scheduler consumes those ideas as part of its scheduled plan ([content-scheduler.ts:782](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:782)). Calling every Muse suggestion-box pick `INTERACTIVE` would let the scheduled planner relabel its own work and starve the schedule.

### 11. [HIGH] Two classes conflate priority, GPU behavior, and job shape

`INTERACTIVE/SCHEDULED` is a priority axis. `render/inference` is a resource-behavior axis. They should not be the same model.

Current job kinds demonstrate why:

- SeedVR2/ESRGAN upscale runs synchronously outside the coordinator queue ([WarmServer.swift:2942](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2942), [WarmServer.swift:3077](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:3077)). It is GPU work but not step-pausable.
- Montage is synchronous, memory-light editorial work and should not consume diffusion capacity ([WarmServer.swift:1061](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:1061)).
- Storyboard is a composite parent whose image/video child jobs deliberately take separate FIFO turns ([WarmServer.swift:4329](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:4329), [WarmServer.swift:3476](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:3476)).
- Face swap is a desktop-only external Python process; the server never sees it ([FaceSwapService.swift:1](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ComfyBoxDesktop/Face/FaceSwapService.swift:1)).

Model at least:

```text
priority: interactive | userBackground | scheduled
resource: gpuDiffusion | gpuNonPausable | gpuInference | cpuMedia | external
preemption: stepBoundary | phaseBoundary | none
shape: atomic | composite
memoryEstimate / runtimeEstimate
```

All GPU paths, especially upscale, must join the same admission broker before the lease guarantee can claim “ANY model sharing the GPU.” Face swap cannot be reserved or centrally displayed until it is brokered by the server.

### 12. [HIGH] A persisted ledger does not provide exactly-once execution

The daemon render queue is explicitly in-memory and loses pending jobs on restart ([render-queue.ts:1](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/media-pool/render-queue.ts:1)). WarmServer persistence only recovers `generate` and `lora_swap`; local video, ControlNet, model switches, and composites are not serializable ([QueuePersistence.swift:9](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/QueuePersistence.swift:9)).

A ledger cannot safely infer whether a vanished submission should be retried without a durable, shared identity. Otherwise restart yields either duplicate media or lost reservations.

Add a reservation state machine:

```text
reserved → dispatching → engineQueued → running → succeeded|failed|cancelled|lost
```

Requirements:

- Stable `reservationId` accepted idempotently by the engine.
- The same ID in queue rows, tracker status, trace, gallery metadata and delivery journal.
- Atomic state persistence before and after submission.
- Reconcile by ID, not merely by discovering an output file.
- Explicit retry/at-least-once policy per job kind.

### 13. [HIGH] The proposed P1 cutover can double-run or skip the 24/7 stream

The current scheduler uses a process-relative `setInterval`, with the first tick five seconds after startup ([content-scheduler.ts:503](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:503)). A persisted slot ledger needs stable slot IDs anchored to a defined timezone and epoch; otherwise a restart can create a second “current cycle.”

The pause sentinel only prevents future ticks. It does not retract an already-emitted scheduler event or pending low-priority callbacks. There is also historical evidence of double-started scheduler timers, guarded only by an in-process flag ([content-scheduler.ts:506](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/content-scheduler.ts:506)).

Safe migration sequence:

1. Fix the policy/FPS/job-ID contracts.
2. Deploy a shadow planner that persists proposed slots but submits nothing.
3. Compare shadow output with current tick behavior, including rotation, neutral film, mixed-mode resolution and unlimited filler.
4. Fence the old scheduled emitter with a durable scheduler generation/owner ID.
5. Stop only new scheduled emission; keep interactive traffic alive.
6. Drain or reconcile already-emitted daemon and engine work.
7. Activate one ledger slot at the next stable boundary.
8. Keep a rollback switch that resumes the old tick without replaying the same slot.

### 14. [HIGH] The desktop needs four strata, and current APIs cannot supply them

The real execution stack has:

1. Running in engine.
2. Pending in engine.
3. Pending in the daemon’s local priority queue.
4. Reserved in the scheduler ledger.

The draft omits stratum 3, where most scheduled composite callbacks and fast-lane jobs wait before engine submission.

Current `/v1/queue` exposes only ID, kind, summary, source and enqueue time for pending work; the active row does not even include kind ([WarmServer.swift:2320](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:2320)). It has no priority, MediaType, reservation ID, estimate, deadline, parent job, or capabilities. The desktop mirrors that limited shape exactly ([EngineService.swift:1270](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ComfyBoxDesktop/EngineService.swift:1270)).

Local video operations already appear in the engine pending list as generic `kind: video` ([WarmServer.swift:5219](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/zimage.swift/Sources/ZImage/Server/WarmServer.swift:5219)); the draft’s “make video jobs appear” premise is therefore stale. What remains invisible is:

- Video work waiting in the daemon queue.
- Cloud video.
- Storyboard parent state.
- Montage and upscale.
- Desktop-local face swap.
- Reservation metadata.
- Public tracker-to-queue ID correlation.

`kira-api.ts` currently has only status/pause/resume/policy scheduler routes—no ledger endpoint or mutation routes ([kira-api.ts:7](/Users/toddwalderman/Projects/coffeeshop-server/src/kira/api/kira-api.ts:7)). A GET ledger alone also cannot implement the promised push/pull/cancel controls.

Define a versioned aggregate DTO or make the desktop explicitly join both services. It needs:

- `reservationId`, `engineJobId`, `daemonJobId`, parent/child IDs.
- `stratum`, priority, resource class, MediaType/revision.
- Planned/start/deadline timestamps and conservative estimate.
- Phase/progress and lease state.
- Per-row allowed actions.
- Versioned mutation endpoints with conflict detection.

## Recommended fold-in sequence

1. Lease/quiescence protocol and phase-aware memory admission: findings 1–3.
2. End-to-end atomic priority and truthful SLA: findings 4–5.
3. Repair FPS and policy contracts before storing new configuration: findings 6–7.
4. Replace additive-median packing with stateful conservative planning: findings 8–10.
5. Generalize resource/job taxonomy and durability: findings 11–12.
6. Specify shadow cutover and only then build the desktop aggregation surface: findings 13–14.

This was a read-only review; no code or tests were changed.
